### PREFACE
# BY MR. MICHEL NOSTRA-
## DAMUS TO HIS
## Prophecies.
### To Cesar Nostradamus, his son <note>original: "Ad Cæsarem Nostradamum filium"</note>
### Life and happiness.

Your late arrival, Cesar Nostradamus my son, has led me to spend a long time in continual nightly vigils <note>original: "vigilations nocturnes"</note> to record in writing and leave you a memory, after the bodily death of your father <note>original: "progeniteur"</note>, for the common benefit of mankind, regarding those things which the divine essence has given me knowledge of through astronomical revolutions. And since it has pleased the immortal God that you have only recently come into the natural light of this earthly shore <note>original: "terriene plaige"</note>—and I do not wish to speak of your years, which are not yet many, but of your infant months <note>original: "mois Martiaux," likely referring to the month of March or the astrological influence of Mars</note> which are incapable of receiving in your weak understanding <term>debile entendement</term> <note>a Renaissance term for the undeveloped intellect of a child</note> what I will be compelled to define after my days are ended: seeing that it is not possible to leave you in writing what would be obliterated by the injury of time—for the hereditary word of secret prediction will be locked within my breast <note>original: "estomach intercluse"; in this period "estomach" often referred to the heart or the seat of secret thoughts</note>—considering also that future events are definitely uncertain, and that everything is ruled and governed by the inestimable power of God, who inspires...

<summary>This is the opening of Nostradamus's dedication to his son Cesar, explaining his motivation for recording his prophecies: a desire to pass on his divinely-inspired astronomical knowledge before his death.</summary>
<keywords>Nostradamus, Cesar Nostradamus, preface, astrology, prophecy, divine inspiration, legacy, father and son</keywords>

*movement: but by astronomical assertions,* **Only those inspired by divine power and a prophetic spirit predict specific things.** <note>original: "Soli numine diuino afflati præsagiunt, & spiritu prophetico, particularia."</note> *Although for a long time and on several occasions I have predicted, long in advance, that which has since come to pass, and in specific regions—attributing it all to divine power and inspiration—and other fortunate and sinister adventures pronounced with sudden promptness, which have since occurred throughout the various climates of the world: I have wished to remain silent and cease from putting them in writing, because of the injury not only of the present time, but also of the greater part of the future. This is because the kingdoms, sects, and regions will undergo changes so opposite—indeed, diametrically so with respect to the present—that if I were to relate what will happen in the future, those of the [present] kingdom, sect, religion, and faith would find it so poorly matching their preconceived notions <note>original: "fantasie auriculaire" — literally "ear-fancy," referring to what people are accustomed to hearing or what they want to hear</note> that they would come to condemn what in future ages will be recognized as having been seen and perceived. Considering also the sentence of the true Savior:*

> **Do not give that which is holy to the dogs, nor cast your pearls before swine, lest they trample them under their feet and turn and tear you to pieces.** <note>original: "Nolite sanctum dare cantibus..." — The OCR "cantibus" (songs) is likely a typesetter's error for "canibus" (dogs) in the Latin Vulgate, Matthew 7:6.</note>

*This has been the cause of making me withdraw my tongue from the common people, and my pen from the paper. Later, I wished to express myself by declaring for the common advent <note>the future arrival of events</note>, through obscure and perplexing sentences, the future causes—even the most urgent ones, and those which I have perceived, [despite] any human mu-*

...entirely prophetic, although, **"You have hidden these things from the wise and the prudent—that is, the powerful and kings—and have explained them to the small and the weak."** <note>original Latin: "Abscondisti hæc à sapientibus, & prudentibus, id est potentibus & regibus, & enucleasti ea exiguis & tenuibus"</note> And through these prophecies, by the means of the immortal God and the good Angels, they have received the spirit of <term>vaticination</term> <note>the act of prophesying or foretelling the future</note>, through which they see distant things and come to foresee future events. For nothing can be brought to completion without Him, whose power and goodness toward His subjects are so great that while He remains within them, nevertheless—for other effects subject to the similarity of the cause of the <term>Good Genius</term> <note>a guiding spirit or divine spark, often used in a Renaissance context to mean a protective or inspiring supernatural force</note>—this prophetic heat and power approaches us. This occurs much like the rays of the Sun, which cast their influences upon <term>elementary bodies</term> <note>physical objects made of the four elements: earth, air, fire, and water</note> and non-elementary bodies alike.

As for us humans, we can know nothing of the hidden secrets of God the Creator through our own natural knowledge or the inclination of our wit. **"For it is not for us to know the times or the moments, etc."** <note>original Latin: "Quia non est nostrum noscere tempora, nec momenta, &c." — a reference to the Book of Acts</note> Nevertheless, even at present, there can be and are individuals to whom God the Creator has willed to reveal, through imaginative impressions, certain secrets of the future, aligned with <term>judicial astrology</term> <note>the branch of astrology used to predict earthly events, weather, and the fates of nations, rather than just individual character</note>. As in the past, a certain power and voluntary faculty came through them, appearing like a flame of fire, so that by His inspiration, one came to judge both divine and human inspirations. For God completes divine works, which are totally absolute; the middle way <unclear>being the angels, and the third being the evil spirits.</unclear>

<meta>catchword: les (the)</meta>

<summary>Nostradamus explains that prophecy is a divine gift granted through the "spirit of vaticination," comparing God's influence to the sun's rays affecting the earth. He argues that while humans cannot naturally know the future, God reveals secrets to certain people through imaginative impressions and judicial astrology.</summary>
<keywords>prophecy, vaticination, divine inspiration, judicial astrology, Good Genius, elementary bodies, revelation, God the Creator</keywords>

--- Page 15 ---
<meta>This page continues Nostradamus’s famous preface to his son, César. Having previously explained that human intellect cannot grasp God’s secrets alone, he now begins to describe the specific, almost physical process of his prophetic trances—mentioning the "spirit of fire" and the state of being "vigilant" while the mind is agitated.</meta>

### PREFACE.

But my son, I speak to you here a bit too **obscurely** <note>original: "obstrusement"</note>. But as for the hidden <term>vaticinations</term> <note>prophecies or predictions of the future</note> that one comes to receive through the subtle spirit of fire—which sometimes, through an agitated understanding, contemplates the highest of the Stars while remaining vigilant—as well as the pronouncements: being surprised, I have written them down, pronouncing them without fear and without being affected by **immodest talkativeness** <note>original: "inuerecunde loquacité"; meaning a lack of shame or restraint in speaking</note>. Yet, indeed, all proceeded from the divine power of the great eternal God, from whom all goodness proceeds.

Furthermore, my son, although I have used the name of "Prophet," I do not wish to attribute to myself a title of such high sublimity for the present time. For **"he who is called a Prophet today, was formerly called a seer"** <note>original Latin: "Propheta dicitur hodie, olim vocabatur videns"; a reference to the Bible, 1 Samuel 9:9</note>. For properly speaking, my son, a Prophet is one who sees things that are far removed from the natural knowledge of any creature. 

And should it happen that, through the perfect light of prophecy, divine as well as human things appear clearly to the Prophet, it cannot be done [otherwise], seeing that the effects of future prediction extend far. For the incomprehensible secrets of God, and the effective power touching upon a long range of natural knowledge—taking their most immediate origin from <term>free will</term> <note>original: "liberal arbitre"; the theological concept that humans choose their actions, which complicates the "fixed" nature of destiny</note>—make causes appear which by themselves cannot be known, neither by human omens, nor by any other knowledge or hidden power contained beneath the concavity of Heaven itself, out of the present fact of total eternity, which comes in faith to embrace all of time.

But by means of some indivisible eternity, and through a <term>Heraclian agitation</term> <note>original: "agitation Hiraclianne." This refers to the "sacred disease" or epilepsy, which was historically associated with Heracles (Hercules) and was often thought in the Renaissance to be a state of divine possession or prophetic frenzy</note>, the...

<summary>Nostradamus explains that his prophecies come from a divine "spirit of fire" and a trance-like state, rather than his own merit. He humbly distances himself from the title of "Prophet," defining it instead as the ability to see things beyond natural human reach through God's light.</summary>
<keywords>vaticination, prophecy, divine inspiration, free will, Heraclian agitation, Seer, astrology</keywords>

--- Page 16 ---
<meta>This page continues Nostradamus’s letter to his son César. On the previous page, he discussed how the Prophet perceives things beyond natural knowledge, originating from God’s power and the exercise of free will. He now transitions into how celestial movements and divine inspiration combine to reveal the future.</meta>

### PREFACE.

...causes by the celestial movement are known. I am not saying, my son—so that you may understand it well—that knowledge of this matter cannot yet be impressed upon your **tender brain** <note>original: "debile cerueau"; in this context, it refers to the unformed or youthful mind of a child rather than a lack of intelligence</note>, nor that distant future causes are beyond the reach of a rational creature. Nevertheless, if the creature is guided by the intellectual soul, things present and distant are not entirely hidden from it, nor too far removed. 

However, the perfect knowledge of these causes cannot be acquired without that **divine inspiration**: seeing that all prophetic inspiration takes its primary moving principle from God the Creator, then from fortune, and from nature. Therefore, since neutral causes are produced indifferently—or not produced at all—the omen partly comes to pass as it was predicted. For the human intellect, created as it is, cannot see hidden things unless by a voice made at the **threshold** <note>original: "lymbe"; likely referring to a liminal state or the "edge" of consciousness where the physical and spiritual meet</note>, by means of a tiny flame toward which part of the future causes will begin to lean.

And also, my son, I beg you never to employ your mind in such fantasies and vanities which wither the body and lead the soul to perdition, troubling the weak senses. This includes the vanity of that most **execrable magic**, condemned long ago by the Holy Scriptures and by divine <term>Canons</term> <note>laws or decrees established by a church council</note>. From this condemnation, however, the judgment of <term>judicial Astrology</term> <note>the practice of making specific predictions about future events or human affairs based on the positions of celestial bodies</note> is excepted. 

It is through this astrology—and by means of divine inspiration and revelation combined with continuous **calculations** <note>original: "supputations"</note>—that we have set down our prophecies in writing. And although that <term>occult Philosophy</term> <note>the study of hidden natural and divine forces, considered a legitimate science by many in the Renaissance</note> was not condemn-

<summary>Nostradamus explains that while the human soul has some capacity to sense the future, true prophecy requires divine inspiration and "judicial astrology" rather than forbidden magic. He warns his son against the "execrable" practices of the dark arts while defending his own calculated, divinely-inspired methods.</summary>
<keywords>divine inspiration, judicial astrology, occult philosophy, prophecy, celestial movement, magic vs. science, liminal state</keywords>

--- Page 17 ---
<meta>This page continues Nostradamus’s address to his son, César, regarding the source of his prophecies. The previous page ended with a warning against "execrable magic," leading into the author's decision to destroy his own occult library to protect his son from such dangerous pursuits.</meta>

### PREFACE.

<sig>A 5</sig>

...professions, although several volumes which had been hidden for long centuries have not been made manifest. But fearing what might happen, after reading them, I made a present of them to **Vulcan** <note>The Roman god of fire; here, Nostradamus uses the name to mean he burned the books</note>, so that while he was devouring them, the flame licking the air gave off an unusual brightness, clearer than a natural flame, like the light of a **flashing lightning bolt** <note>original: "clystrefulgurant"; a rare, archaic term suggesting a brilliant, piercing flash of light</note>, suddenly illuminating the house as if it were in a sudden conflagration. Therefore, so that in the future you would not be deceived, searching for that **perfect transformation** <note>A reference to alchemy, specifically the transmutation of metals into gold or silver</note>, whether **lunar** or **solar** <note>original: "seline que solitaire"; likely intended as "selaine" (lunar) and "solaine" (solar), the two primary branches of alchemy</note>, or seeking **incorruptible metals** beneath the earth or within hidden waves, I have converted them to ashes.

But as for the judgment that is now reaching completion by means of celestial judgment, I wish to reveal this to you: so that you may have knowledge of **future causes**, casting far away those **fantastic imaginations** <note>false visions or delusional thoughts</note> that will occur—defining the specificity of places through supernatural divine inspiration, in accordance with the celestial figures—the places, and a portion of time, with hidden property through divine virtue, power, and faculty. In the presence of this power, the three times—past, present, and future—are comprised by eternity, their revolution holding to the cause of the past, present, and future: **for all things are naked and open, etc.** <note>original: "quia omnia sunt nuda & aperta, &c."; a reference to the biblical passage Hebrews 4:13, meaning nothing is hidden from God</note>. Therefore, my son, you can easily—notwithstanding your **tender brain**—understand that things which must come to pass can be prophesied by the nocturnal and celestial lights, which are natural, and by the spirit of prophecy: not that I wish to attribute to myself the name or

<meta>catchword: qui</meta>

<summary>Nostradamus describes burning his occult and alchemical books to prevent his son from being led astray by "execrable magic," explaining that true prophecy comes from divine inspiration and celestial observation rather than forbidden arts.</summary>
<keywords>Vulcan, book burning, alchemy, transmutation, divine inspiration, celestial judgment, eternity, prophecy, Hebrews 4:13</keywords>

--- Page 18 ---
<meta>This page continues Nostradamus’s personal address to his son, César. After explaining why he destroyed his occult books in the previous section to protect his son from "execrable magic," he now transitions into justifying his own prophetic work as a divine, rather than demonic, gift.</meta>

### PREFACE.

<sig>l</sig>

<meta>catchword: she</meta>

As a mortal man, whose senses are no less distant from Heaven than his feet are from the earth. **I can err, be mistaken, and be deceived** <note>original: "Possum non errare, falli, decipi"; a humble Latin admission of human fallibility common in Renaissance writing</note>, for I am a greater sinner than anyone in this world, subject to all human afflictions. But being occasionally overtaken during the week by a **prophetic trance** <note>original: "limphatiquant"; an archaic term for someone seized by a divine frenzy or "water-madness," implying a state of inspiration</note>, and through long calculation, making my nightly studies yield a sweet fragrance, I have composed books of prophecies. Each contains one hundred **astronomical quatrains** <term>four-line stanzas</term> of prophecies, which I have chosen to assemble somewhat obscurely. These are perpetual **vaticinations** <term>prophetic predictions</term>, extending from this moment until the year 3797.

It is possible that some will **frown or pull back in surprise** <note>original: "retirer le front"</note> upon seeing such a long extension of time, yet everything occurring beneath the **hollow of the Moon** <note>The sublunary sphere, referring to the physical world subject to change and decay, as opposed to the unchanging heavens</note> shall take place and be understood; and this, my son, encompasses causes throughout the entire earth. If you live a natural human lifespan, you will see the fulfillment of these future events foreseen under your own climate and the very sky of your birth.

Although the only Eternal God is the only one who knows the Eternity of His light, proceeding from Himself, I say frankly that to those whom His immense magnitude—which is measureless and incomprehensible—has willed to reveal through long **melancholy inspiration** <note>In the 16th century, "melancholy" was seen not just as sadness, but as the specific temperament required for deep study and divine insight</note>, that by means of this hidden cause divinely manifested, primarily through two main causes which are understood by the inspired person who prophesies. The first is that which comes to infuse, clarifying with **supernatural light**, the person who predicts by the **doctrine of the Stars** <term>astrology</term>, and prophesies...

<summary>Nostradamus admits his human fallibility but claims his prophecies are derived from divine inspiration and astronomical calculation, setting a timeline for his predictions that lasts until the year 3797.</summary>
<keywords>astronomical quatrains, 3797, vaticinations, divine inspiration, sublunary sphere, melancholy, astrology</keywords>

--- Page 19 ---
<meta>This page continues the sentence from the previous page, completing the word "participation." Nostradamus is explaining the dual nature of his prophecy: it is a gift from God ("divine spirit") combined with his own intellectual study of the stars ("natural instinct").</meta>

### PREFACE.

...participation in divine eternity, through which the Prophet comes to judge that which his divine spirit has given him by means of God the Creator, and by a natural instinct. This is to say: that which is predicted is true and has taken its origin **ethereally** <note>original: "ethereement"; meaning originating from the heavens or the celestial spheres</note>. Such a light and small flame is completely effective, and of such height—no less than natural clarity—and this natural light makes the **Philosophers** <note>In the 16th century, "philosophers" often referred to scientists and students of the natural world, including astronomers</note> so confident that, by means of the principles of the First Cause, they have reached the deepest abysses of the highest doctrines.

But to the end, my son, that I do not wander too deeply for the future capacity of your understanding, and also because I find that learning will suffer such a great and incomparable **erasure** <note>original: "rature"; suggesting a loss of records or a "wiping clean" of human knowledge</note>, I find that before the universal **conflagration** <term>a massive, world-consuming fire</term> to come, there shall be so many floods and such high inundations that there will hardly be any land that is not covered by water. This shall last for so long that, except for **hydrographies** <note>original: "enographies"; likely a printer's error for "hydrographies," the mapping of bodies of water</note> and **topographies** <term>the detailed mapping of local physical features of the land</term>, everything shall perish.

Also, both before and after such floods, in many countries, the rains will be so scarce, and such a great abundance of fire and **white-hot stones** <note>original: "pierre candantes"; likely referring to meteor showers or volcanic debris</note> shall fall from the Sky, that nothing will remain that is not consumed. And this shall happen soon, and before the final fire. For even though the planet **Mars** completes its cycle and reaches the end of its last period, it will take it up again; but some planets shall be assembled in **Aquarius** for several years, and others in **Cancer** for even longer and more continuous periods. And now that we are guided by the **Moon**, through...

<summary>Nostradamus warns his son of impending global catastrophes, including massive floods and celestial fires, that will occur before the final end of the world. He justifies his predictions through a mix of divine inspiration and astrological cycles involving Mars, Aquarius, and Cancer.</summary>
<keywords>divine eternity, prophecy, universal conflagration, floods, hydrography, astrology, Mars, Aquarius, Cancer, lunar cycle</keywords>

--- Page 20 ---
<meta>This page continues from the previous page's discussion of environmental catastrophes. It completes the thought that after a period of extreme drought and fire from the heavens has "completed its total circuit," the planetary ages of the Sun and Saturn will begin.</meta>

### PREFACE.

<note>original: "aye paracheué"; continuing from the previous page</note> has completed its total circuit, the Sun will come, and then Saturn. For according to the celestial signs, the reign of Saturn shall return; so that, all things being calculated, the world approaches an **anagonic revolution** <note>original: "anaragonique"; likely a combination of "anagogic," referring to spiritual elevation, and "anachronic." It suggests a mystical cycle or a turning back of time.</note>. From the present moment that I write this, before one hundred seventy-seven years, three months, and eleven days, through pestilence, long famine, and wars, and even more by floods, the world—between now and this fixed term, multiple times both before and after—shall be so diminished, and so few people will remain, that no one will be found to take up the fields, which will become free for as long as they have been in servitude.

And this is according to the visible celestial judgment: even though we are currently in the **seventh number of a thousand** <note>A reference to the millenarian belief that the world would endure for 7,000 years before a final transformation</note> which completes the whole, we are approaching the eighth, where the firmament of the **eighth sphere** <term>In the pre-Copernican model of the universe, the eighth sphere was the layer of the heavens containing the "fixed stars," beyond the seven planetary spheres</term> is located, which is in latitudinal dimension, where the great eternal God will come to complete the revolution. There, the celestial images will return to motion, and the superior movement which makes the earth stable and firm for us **shall not be moved forever and ever** <note>original Latin: "non inclinabitur in saeculum saeculi"; a liturgical phrase from Psalm 104:5, affirming the world's stability until God decrees otherwise</note>; unless His will be accomplished, but not otherwise.

Although through ambiguous opinions exceeding all natural reason, such as **Mahometan dreams** <note>original: "songes Mahometiques"; a common 16th-century derogatory term used by European writers to dismiss visions they considered false, deluded, or non-Christian</note>, also at times God the Creator, through the ministry of His messengers of fire in a sent flame, comes to propose to the external senses—and even to our eyes—the causes of **future prediction** <term>the act of foretelling specific events before they happen</term>, signifying the future event that must be revealed to the one who prophesies. For the prophecy which is made from the ex-
<meta>catchword: terior</meta>

<summary>Nostradamus describes a period of extreme depopulation caused by war, famine, and flood, leading to a mystical "Saturnian" age. He aligns his prophecies with the celestial "eighth sphere" and the end of a 7,000-year cycle of history.</summary>
<keywords>Saturn, eighth sphere, prophecy, depopulation, millenarianism, celestial judgment, divine will</keywords>

<meta>This page continues Nostradamus’s preface to his son, César. It follows a discussion of the world’s cyclical destruction and the transition into the planetary ages of the Sun and Saturn, ending with the assertion that the earth's stability is maintained by divine movement.</meta>

### PREFACE.

<margin>
The speaking of the mind is known both through the light of intellectual understanding and through ignorance [rendered into] a supernatural presence of the mind... God... <unclear/>
<note>original Latin: "mentis locutio, cognoscitur, tum per lumen intellectus intelligentiae, tum per ignorantia his praesentia supernaturaly... mentis... deus..."</note>
</margin>

...and by means of external light; although in truth, for the part that seems to possess through the eye of the understanding that which is not [actually present] due to some injury to the imaginative sense, the reason is all too evident: the whole is predicted through the breath of divinity and by means of the Angelic spirit *inspired in the prophesying man. Anointing him with prophecies, the spirit comes to illuminate him, moving the forefront of his imagination through various nightly apparitions, so that with daytime certainty he prophesies through astronomical administration joined with the most holy future prediction, considering nothing else but a free heart.* 

Come now and understand, my son, what I find through my **revolutions** <term>astrological cycles where planets return to specific positions</term>, which are in accord with revealed inspiration: that the mortal sword now approaches us, through pestilence, through war more horrible than has been seen in three human lifetimes, and through famine. This sword shall fall upon the earth and return there often; for the stars agree with this revolution. And as it was said: **"I will visit their iniquities with an iron rod, and I will strike them with lashes"** <note>original: "Visitabo in virga ferrea iniquitates eorum, & in verberibus percutiam eos"; a paraphrase of Psalm 89:32</note>. For the mercy of God shall not be spread thin for a time, my son, until the majority of my prophecies are fulfilled and have come to be fully realized through their cycles. 

Then, many times during these sinister storms, the Lord shall say: **"I will therefore crush, and I will break, and I will have no mercy"** <note>original: "Conteram ergo... & confringam & non miserebor"</note>. And a thousand other adventures shall happen through floods and continual rains, as I have written more fully in my other Prophecies which are composed...

<summary>Nostradamus explains that his prophetic gift comes from a combination of divine angelic inspiration and astronomical calculation. He warns his son of approaching global calamities, including plague, famine, and war, citing biblical authority for these divine punishments.</summary>

<keywords>divine inspiration, angelic spirit, astrology, prophecy, pestilence, war, famine, divine judgment, revolutions</keywords>

--- Page 22 ---
<meta>This page concludes Nostradamus’s lengthy preface to his son, César. It follows a warning about the divine wrath and the "iron rod" of God’s judgment, transitioning into a final explanation of why the prophecies are written in such an obscure style and a closing paternal blessing.</meta>

### PREFACE.

...[t]ime and the fixed term that those humans who come after will see, recognizing the events that have occurred infallibly, as we have noted through the others, speaking more clearly; although the meanings will be contained under a cloud <note>The author refers to his "cloudy" or cryptic style, intended to hide his meanings from contemporaries while remaining clear to those who witness the events later.</note>. Yet **when ignorance is to be removed** <note>original Latin: "sed quando submouenda erit ignorantia"</note>, the matter will be further enlightened.

Bringing this to a close, my son, take then this gift from your father *Michel Nostradamus*, hoping to explain to you each and every prophecy of the quatrains placed here. Praying to the immortal God that He may grant you a long life in good and prosperous happiness.

*From Salon, this 1st of March, 1555.*

<summary>Nostradamus concludes the introduction to his prophecies, asserting that his cryptic verses will become clear once the events they predict come to pass. He signs off the letter to his son César from his home in Salon-de-Provence.</summary>
<keywords>Nostradamus, prophecy, quatrains, Salon-de-Provence, revelation, 1555, César Nostradamus</keywords>

<meta>This page follows the conclusion of the Preface to César Nostradamus, where the author explained his prophetic method. Having established his credentials and the "cloudy" nature of his visions, he now begins the first set of one hundred prophetic quatrains, known as a "Century."</meta>

# PROPHECIES OF
## Mr. Nostradamus.

### CENTURY I.

Sitting at night in secret study,
Alone, resting upon the brass stool <note>The "brass stool" (*selle d’ærain*) is an allusion to the bronze tripod used by the Pythia, the priestess of the Oracle at Delphi.</note>:
A flickering flame coming out of solitude,
Makes successful that which is not to be believed in vain.

-> II. <-

The wand in hand placed in the middle of the tripod's legs <note>original: "Branches." This continues the imagery of ancient Greek divination rituals.</note>,
With water he moistens both the hem and the foot:
A fear and a voice tremble through the sleeves:
Divine splendor. The divine sits nearby.

-> III. <-

When the litter is overturned by the whirlwind,
And they will have their faces covered by their cloaks:
The republic troubled by new people,
Then whites and reds <note>A likely reference to opposing political or religious factions, such as the Bourbon and Guise families or later revolutionary colors.</note> will judge in reverse.

-> IIII. <-

Throughout the universe there shall be one Monarch,
Who will not be long in peace or life:
Then the fishing bark <note>term: "fishing bark" (*piscature barque*). A traditional metaphor for the Roman Catholic Church, referencing St. Peter the fisherman.</note> will be lost,
It will be ruled to its greatest detriment.

-> V. <-

They will be driven out to wage a long battle,

<summary>The first page of the Prophecies establishes Nostradamus's ritualistic method of divination and presents the first four and a half quatrains, touching on themes of political upheaval and the fate of the Church.</summary>

<header>CENTURY I.</header>

Through the country they will be most heavily oppressed,
Towns and cities will have greater debate:
Carcassonne <note>original: "Carcas."</note> and Narbonne will have their hearts tested.

### V I.

The eye of Ravenna will be forsaken,
When the wings fail at its feet:
The two of Bresse will have established
Turin and Vercelli <note>original: "Versel."</note>, which the French <note>original: "Gaulois." Nostradamus frequently uses "Gauls" to refer to the French people of his time.</note> will trample.

### V I I.

Arrived late, the execution performed,
The wind contrary, letters intercepted on the way:
The conspirators, fourteen <note>original: "xiiiij."</note> of a sect,
Their enterprises brought to an end by the Red-haired One <note>original: "Rousseau." This could refer to a specific individual, a nickname, or perhaps a person of red complexion.</note>.

### V I I I.

How many times will you be captured, city of the sun <note>Often interpreted as a reference to Paris or a symbolic center of power.</note>,
Changing the laws to those that are barbaric and vain:
Your misfortune approaches. You will be more of a tributary,
The great Adriatic <note>original: "Hadrie." This usually refers to the Adriatic Sea or the Republic of Venice.</note> will recover your veins.

### I X

From the East shall come the Punic heart <note>An allusion to North African or Carthaginian influence, often associated with the Ottoman Empire or Barbary pirates in the 16th century.</note>,
To vex the Adriatic and the heirs of Romulus <note>original: "Romulides." A poetic term for the Romans or Italians, as descendants of the founder of Rome.</note>:
Accompanied by the Libyan fleet <note>term: "fleet" (original: "classe"). A Latinism derived from *classis*.</note>,
Malta's <note>original: "Melites."</note> temple and nearby islands left empty.

### X.

A serpent conveyed in a cage of iron,
Where the seven children <note>original: "septains."</note> of the King are held:
The old men and fathers will come out from the depths of hell,
Before seeing their fruit die in death and cries.

### X I.

--- Page 25 ---
<meta>This page continues the first Century of prophecies, picking up from Quatrain X. It moves through a series of predictions concerning Italian and French cities, internal conspiracies against monarchs, and the broader astrological portents of war and religious upheaval that define Nostradamus's vision of the "renewal" of the age.</meta>

# CENTURY I.

The movement of the senses, heart, feet and hands,
Shall be in accord at Naples, Lyon, and Sicily:
Swords, fires, and floods then for the noble Romans,
Drowned, killed, and dead through a weak brain <note>original: "cerueau debile." This suggests a leader whose mental frailty or poor judgment leads to a military or natural catastrophe.</note>.

->XII.<-

Within a short time, a false, fragile brute shall speak,
Promptly elevated from low to high:
Then suddenly <note>original: "istant." From the old French meaning 'issuing forth' or 'instantly.'</note> disloyal and unstable,
He who shall have the government of Verona.

->XIII.<-

The Exiles through wrath and internal hatred,
Shall make a great conspiracy against the King:
Secretly they shall introduce enemies through trickery <note>original: "la mime." This could refer to a theatrical "mime" (deception/acting) or be a variant of "mine" (a tunnel or explosive charge).</note>,
And his own old followers shall rise in sedition against them.

->XIIII.<-

From enslaved people <note>original: "gent esclaue." This often refers generally to those in bondage, but in 16th-century French, it could also specifically denote Slavic peoples.</note>, songs, chants, and petitions,
While Princes and Lords are held captive in prisons:
In the future, by brainless idiots,
These [chants] shall be received as divine prayers.

->XV.<-

Mars <note>The Roman god of war, representing military conflict or the influence of the planet Mars in astrology.</note> threatens us with warlike force,
Seventy times shall blood be shed:
The rise and ruin of the Church <note>original: "l'Ecclesiastique." Referencing the clergy or the institutional power of the Catholic Church.</note>,
And even more for those who wish to hear nothing of them.

->XVI.<-

The Scythe <note>original: "Faux." Symbol of Saturn or death.</note> at the Pond, joined toward Sagittarius,
At its high peak and exaltation:
Plague, famine, death by military hand,
The century approaches its renewal <note>original: "renouation." Nostradamus often speaks of cycles where the world is purged and renewed through hardship.</note>.

# CENTURY I.

->XVII.<-

For forty years the Iris <note>The rainbow. In classical mythology, Iris was the messenger of the gods and the personification of the rainbow.</note> shall not appear,
For forty years it shall be seen every day:
The arid earth shall grow in dryness,
And great floods [shall occur] when it is perceived.

->XVIII.<-

Through Gaulish <note>French.</note> discord and negligence
A passage shall be opened to Muhammad <note>original: "Mahommet." This typically refers to the Ottoman Empire or Islamic forces, seen as a major threat to Europe during the 16th century.</note>:
The land and sea of Siena <note>original: "Senoiſe." Likely referring to the Republic of Siena in Italy, though some interpreters suggest the river Seine.</note> soaked in blood,
The Phocaean port <note>original: "port Phocen." Referring to Marseille, which was founded by Greek colonists from Phocaea.</note> covered with sails and rigging <note>original: "nerfs." Literally 'sinews' or 'nerves,' but in a nautical context, it refers to the ropes and cables of a ship.</note>.

When serpents come to circle the altar <note>original: "l'are." From the Latin 'ara' (altar).</note>,
The Trojan blood <note>original: "sang Troyen." A common 16th-century belief held that the French royalty were descended from the survivors of Troy.</note> vexed by the Spanish:
By them a great number shall be made to perish,
The chief fruit <note>The leader or the prize.</note> hidden in the thicket in the marshes.

Tours, Orléans, Blois, Angers, Reims, and Nantes

Deep white clay nourishes the rock,

In vain, those troubled shall not dare to touch it,
Ignorant that at the bottom the earth is clay-like.

->XXII.<-

That which shall live and have no senses,
Iron shall bring to death through its artifice,

### XXIX.
When the fish that is both terrestrial and aquatic
By a powerful wave shall be cast upon the gravel,
Its strange form, smooth and horrifying,

### XXX.
The foreign ship, through the marine storm,
Shall land near an unknown port:
Notwithstanding the signs of the palm branch <note>The palm branch is a traditional symbol of peace and victory.</note>,

For so many years wars will last in Gaul <note>Gaul: the Latin name for France, often used by Nostradamus to evoke ancient history.</note>,

The Eagle, the Cock, the Moon, the Lion, and the Sun are marked.

### XXXII.
The great Empire shall soon be transferred
To a small place which will soon begin to grow,
A very minor place in a tiny county,
Where in the middle he shall come to set down his scepter.

<header>CENTURY I.</header>

Before the conflict, it serves as an ornament to the French <note>original: "pareure." An ornament or finery, suggesting a deceptive appearance of preparation or vanity before a fight.</note>,
One will take it as a good sign, another as an ambiguous, sinister one,
The weak party will hold it as a good omen,

->XXXV.<-

The young lion shall overcome the old,
In a field of battle through a single duel,
Within a golden cage, he shall pierce his eyes,

While "classes" usually means fleets, in the context of this prophecy it is traditionally interpreted as "clauses" or "coups" (blows/wounds).</note>, then to die a cruel death <note>This quatrain is famously linked to the death of King Henry II in 1559, who died after a jousting accident where a splinter from the young Count of Montgomery's lance pierced his gilded helmet and eye.</note>.

Late shall the Monarch come to repent,

->XXXVII.<-

A little before the Sun sets,
Battle joined, a great people in doubt,
Overthrown, the seaport gives no answer,
Bridge and tomb in two strange places.

->XXXVIII.<-

The Sun and the Eagle shall appear to the victor,
A vain response is assured to the vanquished,
Neither by horn nor cries shall armor be stayed,
Vengeance and peace, if achieved through death in that hour.

->XXXIX.<-

At night in bed, the supreme one is strangled,
For having stayed too long, the blond elect,
By three, the Empire is replaced and exhausted <note>original: "exancle," a word derived from the Latin "exantlare," meaning to draw out or to suffer through to the end.</note>,
He will put to death the letter <note>original: "carte." Can mean a map, a card, or a document/letter.</note>, and the packet left unread.

<header>CENTURY I.</header>

By sects, the world confused and schismatic.

### XLVI.

Very near Auch, Lectoure, and Mirande,
Great fire from the sky will fall for three nights:
The cause that occurs will be most stupendous and marvelous <note>original: "Mirande." A play on words between the town name and the Latin *miranda*, meaning "marvelous" or "wondrous."</note>,
Very shortly after, the earth will tremble.

### XLVII.

From Lake Geneva <note>original: "lac Leman."</note> the sermons <note>The OCR reads "fermons," but in the context of the Reformation in Geneva, "sermons" is the likely intended word, referring to the preaching of John Calvin.</note> will annoy,
Days will be reduced by weeks,
Then months, then a year, then all shall fail;
The Magistrates will condemn their vain laws.

### XLVIII.

Twenty years of the Moon's reign having passed,
Seven thousand years another shall hold his monarchy;
When the sun takes up its weary days,
Then my prophecy is fulfilled and finished <note>original: "mine." Likely a truncated form of "termine" (ends/finishes) or a reference to the source/mine of his visions.</note>.

### XLIX.

Long before such intrigues,
Those of the East, through the power of the Moon <note>A reference to the "Lunar virtue," often associated by 16th-century writers with the Ottoman Empire or Islamic world.</note>:
In the year seventeen hundred they will make great captures <note>original: "emmenées." Referring to people or goods carried off as spoils of war.</note>,
Subjugating nearly the entire Northern <note>original: "Aquilonaire." From *Aquilo*, the North wind.</note> corner.

### L.

From the aquatic triplicity <note>A technical term in astrology referring to the three water signs: Cancer, Scorpio, and Pisces.</note> he shall be born,
Of one who will take Thursday as his feast day,
His fame, praise, reign, and power shall grow,
By land and sea, a storm to the Easterners.

### LI.

The heads of Aries <note>The first sign of the zodiac, the Ram, associated with Mars and new beginnings.</note>, Jupiter, and Saturn,
Eternal God, what changes!

<keywords>Gascony, Lake Geneva, astrology, Moon's reign, year 1700, aquatic triplicity, Aries, Jupiter, Saturn</keywords>

<header>CENTURY I.</header>

Then after a long century, its wicked time returns
To Gaul and Italy, what great commotions.

->LII.<-

The two malefic planets joined in Scorpio,
The Great Lord <note>original: "grand Seigneur." Historically, this term often referred to the Sultan of the Ottoman Empire.</note> murdered inside the hall:
A plague <note>original: "Peste." In this context, it could signify a literal epidemic or a metaphorical spiritual corruption.</note> upon the Church brought by the new King,
Lower and Northern Europe.

->LIII.<-

Alas, one shall see a great people tormented,
And the holy law in total ruin,
Throughout all Christendom, other laws will prevail,
When a new mine of gold and silver is found.

->LIIII.<-

Two revolutions made by the wicked scythe-bearer <note>original: "sacigere." Derived from the Latin *sacifer*, an epithet for the god Saturn, who is traditionally depicted with a scythe and associated with time and harvest.</note>,
Making a change <note>original: "permutation."</note> of reign and centuries:
The movable sign <note>original: "mobil signe." An astrological term for the cardinal signs—Aries, Cancer, Libra, and Capricorn—which mark the beginning of the seasons and are associated with change and action.</note> thrusts into its place,
To the two equal in nature and inclination.

->LV.<-

Under the opposite Babylonian climate,
Great shall be the shedding of blood,
Earth and sea, air and sky shall be unjust,
Sects, famine, kingdoms, plagues, and confusion.

->LVI.<-

Sooner and later, you will see great burdens made,
Extreme horrors and vengeances <note>original: "vindications."</note>.
For if the Moon is guided by its angel,
The heavens approach their final inclinations.

->LVII.<-

Through great discord, the trumpet shall shake,

<header>CENTURY I.</header>

Treaty broken, lifting the remainder to Heaven,
A bloody mouth will swim in blood,
The face on the ground anointed with milk and honey.

### LVIII.

Sliced from the belly, one will be born with two heads,
And four arms, it will live for several full years:
On the day that the Eagle-Law <note>original: "Alquiloye." A cryptic word likely blending the Latin "Aquila" (Eagle) and the French "Loi" (Law), often interpreted by scholars as a reference to imperial decrees or the Bourbon dynasty.</note> celebrates its festivals,
Fossano, Turin, and the leader of Ferrara shall follow.

### LIX.

The exiles deported to the Islands,
At the change to a more cruel Monarch,
Will be murdered, and two sparks set among them,
Who were not sparing <note>original: "parques." Derived from the Latin "parcus," meaning frugal or thrifty; here it suggests they were not restrained in their speech.</note> in their speech.

### LX.

An Emperor shall be born near Italy,
Who will be seen as costing the empire very dearly;
They will say, seeing the people with whom he allies himself,
That he will be found less a prince than a butcher.

### LXI.

The republic, miserable and unhappy <note>original: "infelice." From the Latin "infelix," meaning wretched or unlucky.</note>,
Will be devastated by the new magistrate;
Their great gathering from the harmful exile
Will make the Swabians <note>original: "Sueue." Referring to the people of Swabia, a historical region in southwestern Germany.</note> break their great contract.

### LXII.

Alas, what a great loss learning <note>original: "les lettres." In the Renaissance context, this refers to the "humanities" or the collective body of literature and knowledge.</note> will suffer,
Before the cycle of Latona <note>original: "Latona." In Roman mythology, the mother of Apollo and Diana, often associated with the moon. This likely refers to a lunar or astrological cycle.</note> is completed:
Fire and great flood, more through ignorant rulers <note>original: "ignares sceptres." Literally "ignorant scepters," symbolizing kings who lack wisdom.</note>,
Than will be seen restored for a long century.

<header>CENTURY I.</header>

### LXIII.

> When the flowers have passed, the world diminishes,
> For a long time, peace and uninhabited lands;
> One shall travel through the sky, land, sea, and wave,
> Then, wars shall be stirred up once anew.

### LXIIII.

> At night, they thought they had seen the Sun,
> When the pig half-man <note>original: "pourceau demy homme." This likely refers to a "monstrous birth"—a common omen in the Renaissance—or a person of beastly character.</note> shall be seen;
> Noise, singing, and a battle perceived fighting in the Sky,
> And brute beasts shall be heard to speak.

### LXV.

> Children without hands, never was such great lightning seen,
> The Royal child wounded at a game of tennis <note>original: "ieu d’oeſteuf." This refers to "jeu de paume," a precursor to modern tennis, which was a popular but sometimes dangerous pastime for the French nobility.</note>,
> At the well, broken lightning strikes as if to grind,
> Three trussed up by their middles under the chains.

### LXVI.

> The one who shall then carry the news,
> After a moment, he will come to take breath;
> Viviers, Tournon, Montferrand, and Pradelles,
> Hail and storm shall make him sigh.

### LXVII.

> The great famine which I feel approaching,
> Often shifting, then becoming universal,
> So great and long-lasting that they will come to tear
> Roots from the woods, and the infant from the breast.

### LXVIII.

> Oh, what a horrible and wretched torment,
> Three innocents who will be delivered up;
> Poison <note>original: "Poiſſon." While this literally translates to "fish," in the context of "torment" and "treachery," it is frequently interpreted as a variant spelling of "poison."</note> suspected, treacherously and poorly guarded,

<header>CENTURY I.</header>

The black frizzy hair shall bring harm to the Empire,
The bronze-colored beard <note>original: "Barbe d’ærain." Likely a nickname for a specific historical figure or a reference to a bronze statue.</note> shall roast itself on a spit.

### LXXV.
The tyrant of Siena shall occupy Savona,
The captured fortress shall encounter the maritime fleet:
Between the two armies by the march of Ancona <note>Ancona is a port city on the Adriatic coast of Italy.</note>,
Through terror the leader examines them.

### LXXVI.
He shall be uttered with such a fierce name,
That the three sisters <note>original: "trois sœurs." A reference to the Moirai or Fates of classical mythology who spin, measure, and cut the thread of life.</note> shall have destined <note>original: "fato," from the Latin for "by fate."</note> the name:
Then a great people shall say by word and deed,
More than any other he shall have fame and renown.

### LXXVII.
Between two seas he shall raise a promontory,
He who shall later die by the bite of a horse:
His Neptune <note>In this context, "Neptune" refers to a naval commander or the fleet itself.</note> shall fold the black sail,
By Calpe <note>original: "Calpre." The ancient name for the Rock of Gibraltar.</note> and the fleet near Rocheval.

### LXXVIII.
From an old leader shall be born a dull mind,
Degenerating in knowledge and in arms:
The leader of France feared by his sister,
Fields divided, granted to the soldiers <note>original: "gendarmes." In the 16th century, these were elite heavy cavalry units, the "men-at-arms" of the French crown.</note>.

### LXXIX.
Bazas, Lectoure, Condom, Auch, Agen,
Stirred by laws, dispute, and monopoly:
For Bordeaux, Toulouse, [and] Bayonne shall be brought to ruin,
Wishing to renew their Tauropolia <note>original: "tauropole." A reference to the ancient Greek worship of Artemis Tauropolos, which historically involved bull-leaping or bull sacrifice.</note>.

### LXXX.
From the sixth bright celestial splendor,

<header>CENTURY I.</header>

Thunder shall come so strongly in Burgundy,
Then a monster shall be born of a most hideous beast,
March, April, May, June, great strife and disease. <note>original: "charpin & rõgne." "Charpin" likely refers to shredding or lint (metaphorically, being torn apart), while "rogne" refers to the itch or mange, often used to describe social unrest or a literal plague.</note>

->LXXXI.<-

Nine shall be set apart from the human flock,
Separated from judgment and counsel,
Their fate shall be divided upon departure,
Kappa, Theta, Lambda <note>Nostradamus uses the names of Greek letters here, possibly as a cipher for names or specific groups.</note> dead, banished, and scattered.

->LXXXII.<-

When the columns of wood tremble greatly,
Led by the South Wind <note>original: "Austere," likely from the Latin "Auster," meaning the South Wind.</note>, covered with red, <note>original: "rubriche." This can mean a red ochre pigment, a legal heading, or even a religious rubric.</note>
A great assembly shall empty itself out,
Vienna and the land of Austria shall tremble.

->LXXXIII.<-

The foreign nation shall divide the spoils,
Saturn in Mars <note>An astrological reference indicating a period of tension or misfortune.</note> casts its furious gaze,
Horrible and strange to the Tuscans and Latins,
Greeks who shall be eager to strike.

->LXXXIIII.<-

The Moon obscured in deep darkness,
Her brother <note>The Sun.</note> passes with a rusty color;
The great one hidden for a long time in the shadows,
Shall warm the iron in the bloody rain. <note>original: "Tie dera fer." This suggests tempering or heating a blade in preparation for war during an omen of red rain.</note>

->LXXXV.<-

By the Lady's response, the King is troubled,
Ambassadors shall despise their lives,
The great one shall act for his brothers twofold,
Through two shall die ire, hatred, and envy.

<header>CENTURY I.</header>

### LXXXVI.

The great Queen when she sees herself defeated,
Will show an excess of masculine courage,
Upon a horse, she will cross the river completely naked,
Pursued by the sword, it will be an outrage to her person. <note>original: "Suite par fer, à ſoy ſera outrage." This quatrain is often associated by later historians with Mary, Queen of Scots, or other embattled female sovereigns of the 16th century.</note>

### LXXXVII.

By the fire of the Earth-shaker <note>original: "En noſigée" (Ennosigée), from the Greek *Ennosigaios*, an epithet for the god Neptune/Poseidon, referring to earthquakes.</note> from the center of the earth,
The ground will tremble around the "New City," <note>original: "cite neuue." This could refer to Naples (Neapolis) or any city named Villeneuve or Newtown.</note>
Two great rocks will make war for a long time,
Then Arethusa <note>A mythological nymph who turned into a fountain; here likely representing a specific spring or water source.</note> shall redden a new river.

### LXXXVIII.

The divine malady <note>original: "Le diuin mal." Traditionally refers to epilepsy, which was called the "sacred disease" in antiquity, or perhaps a sudden stroke.</note> shall surprise the great Prince,
A short time before he will have married a woman,
His support and credit will suddenly become thin,
Counsel shall die because of the shaved head. <note>original: "la teſte raſée." A common reference in the Centuries to the tonsure of a monk or a high-ranking member of the clergy.</note>

### LXXXIX.

All those from Lérida <note>original: "Illerde," the Latin name for the Spanish city of Lleida.</note> shall be in the Moselle,
Putting to death all those from the Loire and Seine,
The naval path shall come near the high town, <note>original: "hauteuelle." This may refer to a specific place name like Hauteville, or literally a town on a height.</note>
When the Spaniard shall open every vein.

### XC.

Bordeaux, Poitiers at the sound of the bell, <note>original: "au ſon de la campagne." In the dialect of the time, "campana" referred to a large bell.</note>
With a great fleet <note>term: "claſſe" (from the Latin *classis*, meaning a naval fleet).</term> it will go as far as Langon,
Against the Gauls shall be their north wind, <note>original: "tramontane." A cold wind from the north/mountains; metaphorically, a guiding or opposing force.</note>
When a hideous monster shall be born near Orgon.

### XCI.

The Gods will make it appear to humans,
That they shall be the authors of a great conflict,
Before the sky was seen serene, sword and lance, <note>The text ends mid-sentence, describing celestial omens or weapons appearing in the clouds before a storm or battle.</note>

<header>CENTURY I.</header>

# That towards the left hand there will be greater affection

->XCII.<-

Under one ruler, peace will be proclaimed everywhere;
But not for long, there will be looting and rebellion,
Because of a refusal, city, land, and sea will be breached,
A third of a million dead and taken captive.

->XCIII.<-

The Italian land near the mountains will tremble,
The Lion and the Cock <note>The "Lion" often represents Spain, Venice, or the Netherlands, while the "Cock" (Gallus) is the traditional symbol of France.</note> not very well allied,
Out of fear, they will help one another,
Only the Catalan and the moderate Celts <note>original: "Celtes." Nostradamus often uses "Celts" to refer to the French people.</note> remain.

->XCIIII.<-

At Port Selin <note>original: "port Selin." Likely referring to a port under the Ottoman Empire, as "Selin" is derived from the Greek *Selene* (Moon), the symbol of the Crescent.</note> the tyrant is put to death,
Liberty, however, is not recovered:
The new Mars <note>The Roman god of war, here used as a <term>metonym</term> for a new military leader or a state of war.</note> through vengeance and remorse,
The Lady honored by force of terror.

->XCV.<-

Before a monastery, twin children are found,
Of heroic blood, of a monk, and of ancient lineage:
His fame through his sect, speech, and power,
They shall say that the surviving twin <note>original: "vopiſque" (vopiscus). A Latin term for a twin born alive after the other twin has died in the womb.</note> is highly raised.

->XCVI.<-

He who will have the task of destroying
Temples and sects, changed by whim:
He will harm the rocks more than the living,
By a tongue adorned, ears are satiated. <note>This suggests a leader who uses powerful oratory to encourage the destruction of religious monuments (the "rocks") rather than focusing on the people.</note>

->XCVII.<-

That which the sword and flame <note>original: "flemme," likely a variant of *flamme*.</note> could not complete,
The sweet tongue in council will come to achieve:

<header>CENTURY I</header>

Through rest and dreams, the King will wander in thought,
While the enemy is amidst fire and military blood.

->XCVIII.<-

The leader who will have led an infinite number of people
Far from their own skies; of strange customs and language,
Five thousand shall meet their end in Crete and Thessaly,
The fleeing leader saved in a granary by the sea <note>original: "marine grange." This could refer to a specific coastal storehouse or perhaps a ship shaped like a barn.</note>.

->XCIX.<-

The great monarch who will keep company
With two Kings united by friendship:
Oh, what a sigh the great household <note>original: "mefgnie." An archaic French term for a royal retinue or domestic household.</note> will let out,
The children of Narbonne <note>A city in Southern France.</note> all around, what a pity.

->C.<-

For a long time in the sky a grey bird will be seen,
Near Dole <note>A town in the Franche-Comté region of France.</note> and the Tuscan land:
Holding a green branch in its beak,
Soon a great man will die and the war will end.

This page begins with the final line of Quatrain VII before moving into Quatrains VIII through XIII, which largely focus on religious reformation, the fall of monastic orders, and the rise and fall of specific rulers.</meta>

<header>CENTURY II.</header>
<sig>C</sig>

For them a new King, a new edict he forges. <note>original: "neuf Roy." This could mean "nine kings" or "a new king." Given the "new edict," it likely refers to a fresh administration or a specific legislative change.</note>

->VIII.<-

> Sacred temples of the original Roman style,
> Will reject the heavy foundations,
> Taking up their first and human laws,
> Chasing away, not all, but the cult-like worship of saints. <note>This quatrain is often interpreted as a prediction of the Protestant Reformation, which sought to return to the "primitive" church and simplified the veneration of saints.</note>

->IX.<-

> For nine years the thin man will hold the reign in peace,
> Then he will fall into such a bloodthirsty thirst,
> Because of him a great people without faith or law will die,
> Killed by one who is much more gracious. <note>original: "de bonnaire," likely meaning debonair, kind, or of good lineage.</note>

->X.<-

> Before long everything will be set in order,
> We expect a very sinister <term>senestre: Latin for "left," but in augury it denotes a bad omen or an unlucky direction.</term> century:
> The state of the masked and the solitary ones much changed, <note>original: "des masques & des seuls." This likely refers to those in religious orders—the "solitary ones" being monks (from the Greek *monachos*).</note>
> Few will find that they wish to stay in their rank.

->XI.<-

> The next son of the elder will succeed,
> Elevated so high into the realm of the strong;
> Everyone will fear his harsh glory,
> But his children will be thrown out of the kingdom.

->XII.<-

> Eyes closed, opened by ancient fantasy,
> The habits of the solitary ones <note>Referring again to the clothing of monks.</note> will be brought to nothing:
> The great monarch will punish their frenzy,
> Seizing the treasure from the temples in their presence.

->XIII.<-

> The body without a soul no longer being a sacrifice,
> The day of death placed as a birth:

<summary>This page contains Quatrains 8 through 13 of Century II, focusing heavily on themes of religious upheaval, the dissolution of monastic orders, and the volatile transitions of power between monarchs and their heirs.</summary>
<keywords>Reformation, monasticism, religious treasure, royal succession, sinister century, Roman style temples</keywords>

### CENTURY II.

<margin>

</margin>
The divine spirit will make the soul happy,
Seeing the Word in its eternity.
<note>original: "le verbe." This refers to the Logos, the divine Word or the second person of the Trinity in Christian theology.</note>

->XIV.<-

<margin>
Meadows, house
Famine, plague
</margin>
At Tours, Gien, searching eyes will be kept on guard,
They will discover from afar the great siren:
She and her flight will enter the port,
Combat engaged, sovereign power.
<note>original: "grand sereine." This could refer to a "siren" (the mythological creature) or a "great calm/serenity." Given the context of a port and a flight, it suggests a naval or coastal threat. Tours and Gien are French cities located on the Loire river.</note>

->XV.<-

<margin>
Brothers and C
Found them[selves]
The conv[ents]
Displea[sure]

The public wealth emptied by land and sea,
Pisa, Asti, Ferrara, Turin, land interdicted.
<note>original: "astre crinite." A Latinate term meaning a "maned star," or a comet. Castor and Pollux are the Gemini twins, often associated in maritime lore with St. Elmo’s fire.</note>
<term>interdicted: An ecclesiastical censure (interdict) where a city or region is prohibited from participating in most church rites and sacraments.</term>

Dear friend
From the win[dow]

New tyrants, lightning, celestial fires:
Force from London, Ghent, Brussels, and Susa,
A great slaughter, triumph, holding festivities.
<note>original: "hecatombe." Historically a sacrifice of a hundred oxen, used here to mean a massive loss of life or a massacre.</note>

From Arto[is]
Numb[er]

<meta>This page continues the Second Century of Nostradamus's prophecies. The previous page (ending with Quatrain XVI) transitioned into a series of geopolitical and maritime omens. This page picks up with the conclusion of an earlier quatrain—likely XIX—and proceeds through Quatrain XXIV, focusing on naval conflicts in the Mediterranean and the famous "Hister" prophecy.</meta>

# CENTURY II.

To occupy the place [which is] then uninhabitable,
Meadows, houses, fields, towns, taken at pleasure;
Hunger, plague, war, a long stretch of arable land.

### XX.

Brothers and sisters captive in various places,
Will find themselves passing near the monarch:
His attentive branches will contemplate them,
Displeased to see the marks on chin, forehead, and nose.
<note>original: "rameaux." Literally "branches," this term is frequently used in these prophecies to refer to the "offshoots" or children of a royal lineage.</note>

### XXI.

The ambassador sent by biremes,
Halfway on the path repulsed by unknowns:
With reinforcement of salt four triremes will come,
Ropes and chains found at the Black Bridge.
<term>biremes: Ancient galleys with two banks of oars.</term>
<term>triremes: Ancient warships with three banks of oars.</term>
<note>original: "Negre pont." This refers to Negroponte, the Venetian name for the island of Euboea in Greece, which was a major site of conflict between Venice and the Ottoman Empire.</note>

### XXII.

The camp of Ascop will leave Europe,
Joining near the submerged island:
Arton’s fleet will fold the phalanx,
At the navel of the world a greater voice substituted.
<note>original: "Ascop." A cryptic term; some commentators suggest it is a reference to "Episcopus" (the Bishop), while others see it as a coded name for a specific military leader.</note>
<note>original: "Nombril du monde." The "navel of the world," a classical reference to Delphi in Greece or sometimes Jerusalem.</note>

### XXIII.

Birds in the palace, chased out by a bird,
Very soon after the prince has arrived:
Although the enemy is repulsed beyond the river,
Seized outside, sustained by the bird's flight.
<note>The "bird" imagery often refers to heraldry (such as the eagle or the fleur-de-lis) or to omens of swift arrival and departure.</note>

### XXIIII.

Fierce beasts with hunger will swim across the rivers,
The greater part of the field will be against the Hister,
Into a cage of iron the great one will be dragged,
When the child of Germany observes nothing.
<note>original: "Hister." In the 16th century, this was the common Latin name for the lower Danube River. However, this quatrain became one of the most famous in the 20th century due to the phonetic similarity between "Hister" and "Hitler," leading many to interpret it as a prophecy of World War II.</note>

<sig>C ii</sig>
<note>This "C ii" is a signature mark, used by early printers to help bookbinders assemble the pages in the correct order.</note>

<summary>This page covers Quatrains XIX through XXIV of the Second Century, detailing scenes of captivity, naval warfare in the Mediterranean (notably near Euboea), and the famous Hister prophecy. The verses blend classical geographical terms with 16th-century geopolitical anxieties regarding the Ottoman Empire and Central Europe.</summary>

<keywords>Hister, Danube, Negroponte, naval warfare, biremes, triremes, Ascop, Germany, royal lineage, omens</keywords>

<meta>This page continues Century II of Nostradamus’s prophecies. The previous page ended mid-sentence during Quatrain XXIII, describing birds in a palace. This page concludes the sequence of the first twenty-five quatrains and continues through Quatrain XXX, shifting focus toward conflicts in Italy, religious suppression, and a fearsome "Oriental" leader.</meta>

# CENTURY II.

<note>This first paragraph is the conclusion of Quatrain XXV, the beginning of which appeared on the previous page.</note>
The foreign guard will betray the fortress,
Hope and shadow of a higher marriage:
The guard deceived, the fort taken in the crush,
Loire, Saône, Rhône, Gard, to death and outrage.
<note>The rivers mentioned—the Loire, Saône, Rhône, and Gard—trace a path through central and southern France, suggesting a widespread military campaign or disaster.</note>

->XXVI.<-

Because of the favor that the city will grant,
To the great man who will soon lose the battlefield,
Fleeing the ranks, the Po and Ticino will pour
With blood, fire, dead, drowned by slashing blows.
<note>original: "Pau Thesin." Referring to the Po and Ticino rivers in Northern Italy, a frequent theater of war during the Renaissance.</note>

->XXVII.<-

The divine word will be struck from the Sky,
It will be able to proceed no further:
The secret of the reporter is stopped up,
So that one will walk over and before [it].
<note>original: "referrant." This likely refers to a herald or a messenger. This quatrain is often interpreted as a prophecy of censorship or the silencing of a religious or prophetic voice.</note>

->XXVIII.<-

The penultimate of the surname of Prophet,
Will take Diana for his day and rest:
He will wander far with a frantic head,
And delivering a great people from taxes.
<note>original: "impos." An archaic term for impositions or taxes. "Diana" may refer to the moon, symbolizing nighttime or a specific person named Diane.</note>

->XXIX.<-

The Oriental will leave his seat,
To cross the Apennine mountains to see Gaul:
He will pierce the Sky, the waters and snow,
And strike everyone with his rod.
<term>Gaul: The Roman name for the region comprising modern-day France.</term>
<note>original: "sa gaule." There is a wordplay here; "Gaule" refers to France, while "gaule" (lowercase) refers to a long pole or rod used for striking.</note>

->XXX.<-

One who [will cause] the infernal gods of Hannibal
To be reborn, terror of mankind:
Never [was there] more horror nor more reported in newspapers,
Than what will come to the Romans through Babel.
<note>original: "Annibal." Hannibal was the Carthaginian general who famously crossed the Alps to attack Rome. Nostradamus often uses his name to symbolize a great invader from the East or North Africa.</note>
<note>original: "Babel." A biblical reference to the Tower of Babel, usually symbolizing confusion, linguistic chaos, or a powerful eastern empire (like the Ottomans).</note>

**Vocabulary:** Century, Gaul, Hannibal, Babel, Romans, Prophet, Diana, Apennines, Po, Ticino, Loire, Saône, Rhône, Gard.

<summary>This page contains Quatrains XXV through XXX of Century II, detailing violent military defeats in France and Italy, the rise of a rod-wielding "Oriental" invader, and the rebirth of "Hannibalic" terrors affecting Rome.</summary>
<keywords>French rivers, Italian campaigns, censorship, taxation, Oriental invader, Hannibal, Rome, Babel, prophecy</keywords>

->CENTURY II.<-
<note>Page signature Ciij indicates this is the third leaf of the third gathering of the printed book.</note>

### XXXI.

In Campania, the <term>Casilinus</term> <note>original: "Cassilin." Referring to the river Volturno (anciently Casilinus) or the city of Casilinum near Capua, Italy.</note> will do so much,
That one will see only water covering the fields:
Before and after the long-lasting rain,
Nothing green will be seen except for the trees.

### XXXII.

Milk, blood, and frogs will rain down in Dalmatia,
Conflict joined, plague near <note>original: "Balennes." Possibly Valona (Vlorë) in Albania or a phonetic rendering of a French location.</note> Balennes,
A great cry will go out through all of <term>Slavonia</term>, <note>original: "Esclauonie." An archaic term for the Slavic regions of the Balkans.</note>
Then a monster will be born near and inside Ravenna.

### XXXIII.

By the torrent that descends from Verona,
At the time it guides its entry into the Po:
A great shipwreck, and no less on the Garonne,
When those of Genoa march through their country.

### XXXIIII.

The senseless wrath of the furious combat,
Will make the iron blade shine at the table among brothers,
To separate them—[the one] wounded, [the] curious—
The fierce duel will come to harm France.
<note>Many historians associate this "duel" with the accidental death of King Henri II in a jousting tournament in 1559, which led to decades of civil war in France.</note>

### XXXV.

In two dwellings fire will take hold at night,
Several inside will be suffocated and roasted:
It will happen near two rivers for one alone,
Sun in Sagittarius and Capricorn, all will be extinguished.
<note>original: "Sol l'Arq, & Caper." These are astrological placements—the Sun entering Sagittarius (late November) and Capricorn (late December)—suggesting the events occur in early winter.</note>

### XXXVI.

The letters of the great Prophet will be seized,
They will fall into the hands of the tyrant,
His enterprises will be to defraud his King.
<note>The source text for this quatrain is incomplete on this page, missing the final line: "But his plunderings will soon surprise him."</note>

<summary>This page contains Quatrains XXXI through XXXVI, featuring vivid imagery of natural disasters in Italy, supernatural omens in the Balkans, and a prophetic warning regarding a royal duel and stolen correspondence.</summary>

<keywords>Campania, Dalmatia, Ravenna, Verona, Po River, Garonne, Genoa, France, Sagittarius, Capricorn, prophecy, omens</keywords>

->CENTURY II.<-

But his plunderings will soon trouble him.
<note>This is the concluding line of Quatrain XXXVI, which began on the previous page.</note>

### XXXVII.

Of this great number that will be sent,
To aid those besieged in the fort,
Plague and famine will devour them all,
Except for seventy who will be routed.
<note>original: "profligez." This term implies being defeated, broken, or cast down.</note>

### XXXVIII.

A great number of the condemned will be made,
When the Monarchs are reconciled:
But one of them will meet such misfortune,
That they will not be joined together in war.
<note>The term "consiliez" suggests a council or a diplomatic reconciliation between rulers that ultimately fails to hold.</note>

### XXXIX.

One year before the Italian conflict,
Germans, Gauls, and Spaniards [striving] for the fort,
The schoolhouse of the republic will fall,
Where, except for a few, [those inside] will be suffocated to death.
<note>original: "Cherra l'escolle maison de republique." "Cherra" is an archaic future tense of the verb "choir" (to fall). This quatrain is often debated by historians as referring to either a literal building collapse or the metaphorical fall of a political system.</note>

### XL.

A little after, with no long interval,
By sea and land a great tumult will be made:
Much greater will be the naval battle,
Fires, animals, which will cause more offense.
<note>original: "insulte." In a Renaissance context, this refers to a physical attack or injury rather than a verbal slight.</note>

### XLI.

The great star will burn for seven days,
The cloud will make two suns appear:
The great mastiff <note>original: "gros matin." Likely a variant of "mâtin," meaning a large watchdog or mastiff. Some interpretations suggest "great morning," but the howling context favors the animal.</note> will howl all night,
When the great pontiff changes his territory.
<note>The "two suns" and "burning star" are frequently interpreted by scholars as a comet, which was historically viewed as a dire omen for the Papacy.</note>

### XLII.

Rooster, dogs, and cats will be glutted on blood,
And from the wound of the tyrant found dead,

<meta>catchword: La (The)</meta>

<summary>This page contains a series of prophetic quatrains detailing military failures, the collapse of a public institution, and celestial omens—including a "burning star" and "two suns"—that coincide with the relocation of the Pope and the death of a tyrant.</summary>
<keywords>sieges, military alliances, celestial omens, papacy, comets, Italian wars, political collapse</keywords>

<meta>This page continues Century II of Nostradamus’s prophecies. The previous page ended during Quatrain XLI, which described a celestial event involving two suns and a "great mastiff." This page completes the final lines of Quatrain XLII before continuing through Quatrains XLIII to XLVIII, focusing on celestial omens (comets), natural disasters (floods of the Po and Tiber), and the symbolic movement of imperial powers.</meta>

->CENTURY II.<-

In place of another, legs and arms broken,
Who had not been able to die a cruel death.
<note>These lines conclude Quatrain XLII, which suggests a botched execution or a body broken on the wheel.</note>

### XLIII.

During the appearance of the hairy star,
The three great princes will be made enemies:
Struck from the sky, peace and earth tremble,
Po and Tiber overflowing, a serpent placed on the shore.
<note>original: "eſtoille cheuelue." In the 16th century, a "hairy star" was the standard term for a comet, often viewed as a dire omen for rulers. "Pau" refers to the River Po and "Timbre" to the River Tiber in Italy.</note>

### XLIIII.

The eagle driven around the tents,
Will be chased by other birds around:
When the noise of trumpets, cymbals, and bells,
Will restore the senses of the senseless lady.
<note>original: "cymbres tube & ſonnaillons." While "cymbres" likely refers to the Cimbrians (an ancient Germanic people), it also evokes the sound of cymbals. The "eagle" traditionally represents the Holy Roman Empire.</note>

### XLV.

Too much the sky weeps for the Androgynous one procreated,
Near the sky human blood shed:
Through death too late a great people is restored,
Late and soon comes the awaited help.
<note>original: "Androgyn." The "Androgynous" or hermaphrodite was frequently cited in Renaissance "books of wonders" as a "monstrous birth" signifying a disruption in the natural and political order.</note>

### XLVI.

After a great human exhaustion, a greater one is prepared,
The Great Mover renews the ages;
Rain, blood, milk, famine, sword, and plague,
In the sky fire is seen, a long running spark.
<note>original: "grand troche" interpreted here as exhaustion or ruin. "Le grand moteur" refers to the "Great Mover," a theological and cosmological term for God or the force that moves the celestial spheres.</note>

### XLVII.

The enemy, a great old grief, dies of poison,
The sovereigns subjugated by infinite numbers:
Stones raining, hidden under the fleece,
Through death, articles are cited in vain.
<note>original: "ſoubs la toiſon." This may refer to the Order of the Golden Fleece or literally to sheepskins used for protection during a storm of "stones" (hail or meteorites).</note>

### XLVIII.

The great force that will pass over the mountains,
<note>original: "copie." From the Latin "copia," meaning a large military host or troop.</note>

<summary>This page lists several quatrains concerning apocalyptic omens, including a comet, monstrous births, and celestial rains of blood and milk. It describes political upheaval among three princes and military movements across the Alps.</summary>
<keywords>comet, Po river, Tiber river, Androgynous birth, Great Mover, famine, plague, military force</keywords>

<meta>This page continues Century II of Nostradamus’s prophecies. The previous page (Quatrains XLII to XLVI) discussed celestial omens like a "hairy star" (comet), the overflowing of the Po and Tiber rivers, and symbolic disruptions in the natural order.
