->CENTURY II<-

<unclear>XLVIII.</unclear>

Saturn in Aquarius, Mars turning from Pisces,
Venus hidden under the heads of Salmon,
Their leader hung by a thread of polemarchs.
<note>original: "Aaq" is an abbreviation for "Aquarius." "Polemars" refers to the Greek *polemarchos*, meaning a military leader or general. The "heads of Salmon" is a cryptic heraldic or symbolic reference often debated by scholars.</note>

### XLIX.

The advisors of the first monopoly,
The conquerors seduced by Malta:
Rhodes, Byzantium acting as their support,
Land will fail those pursuing in flight.
<note>original: "Melite" (Malta) and "Bisance" (Byzantium/Istanbul). This quatrain likely refers to the ongoing struggle between the Ottoman Empire and the Knights of Saint John in the Mediterranean.</note>

### L.

When those of Hainaut, of Ghent, and of Brussels
Shall see the siege laid before Langres,
Behind their flanks there will be cruel wars,
The ancient wound will be worse than enemies.
<note>Hainaut, Ghent, and Brussels were major cities in the Spanish Netherlands (modern-day Belgium). Langres is a fortified city in eastern France. The "ancient wound" likely refers to long-standing religious or territorial schisms.</note>

### LI.

The blood of the just will be lacking in London,
Burned by fireballs in twenty-three the six,
The ancient lady shall fall from her high place,
Several of the same sect shall be slain.
<note>original: "foudres de vingt trois les six." This is famously interpreted as a prophecy of the Great Fire of London in 1666 ("three-twenties and six"). The "ancient lady" may refer to St. Paul's Cathedral or a symbolic figure of the city itself.</note>

### LII.

For several nights the earth shall tremble,
In the spring, two efforts in succession;
Corinth and Ephesus shall swim in the two seas,
War is stirred up by two valiant in combat.
<note>Corinth and Ephesus were ancient Greek cities. "Swim in the two seas" suggests massive flooding or seismic activity affecting coastal regions.</note>

### LIII.

The great plague of the maritime city,
Shall not cease until death is avenged
For the just blood, seized and condemned without crime,
And the great lady outraged by pretense.
<note>original: "cite maritime." This is frequently identified as Marseille, though Venice or London are also possibilities. The quatrain links an epidemic to a divine or karmic retribution for a judicial murder.</note>

<meta>catchword: LIIII.</meta>

<summary>This page contains several of Nostradamus's most famous quatrains, including an astrological configuration in XLVIII and the purported prediction of the Great Fire of London in 1666 in LI.</summary>
<keywords>astrology, Saturn, Mars, Malta, Byzantium, Belgium, London, Great Fire of 1666, plague, earthquakes</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the Second Century of Nostradamus's prophecies. The previous page concluded with the famous Quatrain LI regarding the "blood of the just" in London and the Great Fire. This page moves into broader geopolitical conflicts involving Roman influence, maritime disasters, and the mobilization of the French fleet.</meta>

# CENTURY II.

### LIV.
<note>This quatrain is unnumbered in the source text but follows the sequence as number 54.</note>
By strange folk, and Romans from afar,
Their great city after water much troubled,
A daughter without too different a domain,
Chief taken, the lock not having been picked.
<note>original: "romains longtaine" (distant Romans) and "ferreure... riblée" (lock... picked/forced). This likely refers to a city being taken by stealth or betrayal rather than forced entry.</note>

->LV.<-

In the conflict the great one who was of little worth,
At his end will do a marvelous deed,
While Hadrian will see what was lacking,
In the banquet a dagger to the proud one.
<note>original: "Hadrie" may refer to the Adriatic Sea or a specific leader. "Pongnale" is an archaic form of *poignard* (dagger).</note>

->LVI.<-

What plague and sword could not finish,
Dead in the well, struck from the height of heaven:
The Abbot will die when he sees ruined,
Those of the shipwreck trying to grab the reef.
<note>original: "puyes" (well) and "escueil" (reef/rock). "Sommet du ciel frappé" suggests a bolt of lightning or a divine omen.</note>

->LVII.<-

Before the conflict the great one will fall,
The great one to death, too sudden and lamented,
Born half-perfect, the greater part will swim,
Near the river the ground stained with blood.
<note>original: "Nay mi parfaict" (Born half-perfect) could refer to a premature birth, a physical deformity, or a metaphor for an incomplete legacy.</note>

->LVIII.<-

Without foot or hand, with sharp and strong tooth,
By the globe at the fort of the port and the woolly-one born,
Near the portal of the gatehouse he is transported,
Silenus shines, the little great one led away.
<note>original: "Silenes" refers to the satyr Silenus from Greek mythology, tutor to Bacchus. "Lainé" (woolly/born of wool) is a cryptic reference often debated as referring to a specific heraldic sign or a person.</note>

->LIX.<-

Gallic fleet by support of a great guard,
Of great Neptune, and his trident soldiers,
Provence gnawed to support a great band,
More of Mars in Narbonne by javelins and darts.
<note>original: "Classe" (from the Latin *classis*, meaning fleet). "Mars" is the Roman god of war, here signifying military action. "Narbon" refers to the city of Narbonne in southern France.</note>

<summary>This page contains Quatrains 54 through 59 of Century II, focusing on sudden deaths of leaders, maritime military movements in Provence, and cryptic mythological allusions.</summary>

<header>CENTURY II.</header>

The great commerce of a great Lyon <note>Lyon was a major center for trade and banking in 16th-century France.</note> changed,
The greater part turns into ancient ruin,
Prey to the soldiers by pillaging and harvest:
By the Jura mountains and Swabian <note>original: "Sueue" — referring to Swabia, a region in southwestern Germany.</note> mist.

->L X X X I I I I.<-

Between Campania, Siena, Florence, Tuscany,
For six months and nine days not a drop will rain,
The foreign tongue in the land of Dalmatia <note>A region on the Adriatic coast, primarily in modern-day Croatia.</note>,
Will overrun, devastating the whole land.

->L X X X V<-

The old man with a full beard under the strict law,
At Lyon made over the Celtic Eagle <note>The "Celtic Eagle" likely refers to a French imperial or royal symbol, or perhaps the Holy Roman Empire's influence in Gaul.</note>,
The little-great one perseveres too far,
Noise of arms in the sky, the Ligurian <note>original: "Lygustique" — the sea around Genoa, Italy.</note> sea red.

->L X X X V I.<-

Shipwreck of the fleet <note>original: "classe" — from the Latin 'classis', meaning a fleet of ships.</note> near the Adriatic wave,
The earth trembles, moved by air trapped within the earth,
Egypt shakes with Islamic <note>original: "Mahometique" — referring to the expansion of the Ottoman Empire or Muslim forces.</note> growth,
The Herald is commissioned to surrender while crying out.

->L X X X V I I.<-

Afterward shall come from the far-off countries
A German Prince, onto the golden throne,
The servitude and waters encountered together,
The lady enslaved, her time no longer worshipped.

->L X X X V I I I.<-

The circuit of the great feat ruined,
The seventh name will be that of the fifth;

Aries <note>original: "Mouton" — literally 'sheep' or 'ram', likely referring to the astrological sign of Aries.</note>, Paris <note>original: "Lutece" — the Roman name for Paris.</note>, and Aix will not provide protection.

->LXXXIX.<-

One day the two great masters will be friends,
Their great power will be seen to increase:
The <note>original: "terre neuue" — This may refer to the recently discovered Americas or simply a "newly conquered land."</note> new land will be in its highest state,

->X C I I.<-

Fire the color of gold seen from the sky on earth,

->X C I I I I.<-

Great Pau <note>This could refer to the city of Pau in southern France, or it may be a cryptic reference to a person (like a Pope named Paul).</note> will receive great evil for the Gauls <note>The French people.</note>,
Vain terror to the maritime Lion:
Infinite people will pass by the sea,
Without a quarter of a million escaping.

<header>CENTURY II.</header>

Inhabited places will become uninhabitable,
Because of a great division of lands:
Kingdoms handed over to prudent incompetents,
Then for the great brothers, death and dissension.

->XCVI.<-

A burning torch will be seen in the sky at night,
Near the end and the source of the Rhône <note>The major river flowing through Switzerland and France.</note>,
Famine, sword, relief provided too late,
Persia turns to invade Macedonia.

Roman Pontiff <note>The Pope.</note>, beware of approaching
The city that two rivers water <note>This likely refers to Lyon, France, which sits at the confluence of the Rhône and Saône rivers.</note>,
Your blood will come to be spat out near there,
You and yours when the rose shall bloom.

->XCVIII.<-

He whose face is splattered with blood,
Of the victim close to sacrifice,
Entering Leo <note>The zodiac sign of Leo, associated with mid-summer or a specific astronomical timing.</note>, an omen by prophecy,

->XCIX.<-

Roman territory that the omen interpreted,
Will be too greatly vexed by the Gallic <note>French.</note> people:
But the Celtic nation <note>Another reference to France or the peoples of that region.</note> shall fear the hour,
Boreas <note>The North Wind.</note> had pushed the fleet too far away.

->C.<-

Within the islands, such a horrible tumult:
One will hear nothing but a warlike plot,
So great will be the insult of the predators,
That people will come to join the great league.

# PROPHECIES OF
## Mr. Nostradamus.

### CENTURY III.

I.

After combat and naval battle,
The great Neptune <note>The Roman god of the sea, often representing naval power or the ocean itself.</note> at his highest tower,
The red adversary will turn pale with fear,
Putting the great Ocean in terror.

->II.<-

The divine Word <note>original: "Verbe" — likely referring to the Logos or the creative power of God.</note> will give to the substance,
Including heaven, earth, occult gold in the mystic milk,
Body, soul, spirit having all power,
As much under his feet as at the Celestial seat.

->III.<-

Mars and Mercury, and the silver <note>In astrology and alchemy, silver is the metal associated with the Moon.</note> joined together,
Towards the South <note>original: "Midy" — referring to the south of France or the Mediterranean region.</note> extreme dryness:
At the bottom of Asia one will say the earth trembles,
Corinth and Ephesus <note>Ancient Greek cities in modern-day Greece and Turkey, respectively.</note> then in perplexity.

->IV.<-

When the failure <note>likely an eclipse or an unfavorable astronomical alignment.</note> of the lunaries shall be near,
From one to the other not greatly distant,
Cold, dryness, dangers toward the frontiers,
Even where the oracle had its beginning.

->V.<-

Near and far the failure of two great luminaries <note>The Sun and the Moon.</note>,

### CENTURY III.

Dij <note>This is a signature mark used by early modern printers to keep the pages in the correct order for binding.</note>

Weapons clashing in the sky for a long season,
The tree in the middle of the city fallen:
Sacred verbena <note>original: "Verbine" — likely referring to verbena, a plant used in ancient rituals, or perhaps a cryptic reference to a person or place.</note> pruned, the sword, a firebrand in the face,

By the swelling of the Ebro, Po, Tagus, Tiber, and Rome,

The two great leaders and cities of the Garonne,

->XIII.<-

By lightning in the chest <note>original: "l’arche" — can mean an arch, an ark, or a treasury chest. Given the context of gold and silver, a chest is most likely.</note> gold and silver melted,

->XIV.<-

Of lowest France, by the unhappy father:
Honors, riches, and toil in his old age,
For having believed the advice of a foolish <note>original: "nice" — in Middle French, this meant simple, ignorant, or foolish.</note> man.

->XV.<-

Heart, vigor, and glory, the kingdom shall change,
In all points having its adversary against it:
Then France shall subjugate childhood by death,
A great Regent shall then be more contrary.

->XVI.<-

An English Prince with Mars in his heavenly heart,
Will want to pursue his prosperous fortune:
Of the two duels, one will pierce the gall,
Hated by him, but well loved by the mother.

<header>CENTURY III.</header>

### XVII.

Mount Aventine <note>One of the seven hills of ancient Rome, often used here to symbolize the Papacy or the city of Rome itself.</note> will be seen burning at night,
The sky suddenly darkens over Flanders:
When the Monarch shall chase away his nephew,
Their churchmen shall commit great scandals.

### XVIII.

After a fairly long rain of milk,
In several places around Reims, the sky is touched <note>Likely referring to lightning strikes or a celestial omen over this French city, traditionally the site of royal coronations.</note>:
Oh, what a bloody conflict prepares itself near them,
Father and son Kings will not dare approach.

### XIX.

In Lucca <note>A city in Tuscany, Italy.</note>, blood and milk will fall as rain,
Shortly before a change of praetor <note>original: "preteur" — a high-ranking magistrate or administrative official, a term borrowed from ancient Roman governance.</note>:
Great plague and war, hunger and thirst will be seen
Far away where their Prince-Ruler shall die.

### XX.

Throughout the regions of the great river Baetis <note>original: "Betique" — the Roman name for the Guadalquivir River in southern Spain.</note>,
Far from the Ebro, in the kingdom of Granada,
Crosses pushed back by Mohammedan people <note>original: "gens Mahometiques" — a historical term for Muslims, specifically referring here to the Moors or the Ottoman Empire.</note>,
One from Cordoba shall betray the final district.

### XXI.

At Crustaminum <note>original: "Cruſtamin" — an ancient city in Italy near the Tiber, or perhaps the Conca river which flows into the Adriatic.</note> by the Adriatic Sea,
A horrible fish shall appear,
With a human face and aquatic form,
Which shall be caught without a hook.

### XXII.

For six days the assault is given before the city:
A strong and bitter battle shall be delivered:
Three shall surrender it, and be pardoned,

<note>This quatrain appears incomplete in the original printing, missing its final fourth line.</note>

# CENTURY III.

A young female to reign for a long time,
So that never in a reign did one so bad follow.

->XXIX.<-

The two nephews raised in different places:
Naval battle, on land the fathers fallen:
They will rise to such high war-like stature,
To avenge the injury, enemies succumbed.

->XXX.<-

He who in struggle and iron in warlike deed,
Will have carried away the prize from one greater than himself:

Naked, without armor, he will be suddenly surprised.

In the fields of Media, Arabia, and Armenia,

Of the great Suleiman <note>original: "Soliman" — referring to Suleiman the Magnificent (r. 1520–1566), the Sultan of the Ottoman Empire.</note> will fall on the land.

->XXXII.<-

The great tomb of the Aquitanian people <note>The "Aquitanian people" refers to the inhabitants of Aquitaine in southwestern France.</note>

When Mars shall be near the Germanic corner

->XXXIII.<-

In the city where the wolf shall enter,
Very near there the enemies will be:
A foreign army will waste a great country,
Over the walls and the Alps the friends will pass.

When the failure of the Sun <note>original: "defaut du Soleil" — likely referring to a solar eclipse, often viewed as a dire omen.</note> shall then be,

# CENTURY III.

In plain daylight the monster will be seen,
It will be interpreted quite differently,
High prices <note>original: "Cherté" — typically referring to famine or the skyrocketing cost of grain</note> have no guard, no one will have provided for it.

->XXXV.<-

From the deepest part of Western Europe,
Of poor people a young child will be born,
Who by his tongue <note>Meaning through his speech or oratory skills.</note> will seduce a great troop;
His fame will grow even more in the Eastern realm.

->XXXVI.<-

Buried, though not dead, in an apoplectic state,
He will be found to have his hands eaten,
When the city shall condemn the heretic,
Who had, so it seemed to them, changed their laws.

->XXXVII.<-

Before the assault, the prayer is delivered;
Milan taken, the Eagle <note>The "Eagle" is a common heraldic symbol for the Holy Roman Empire or the Habsburg dynasty.</note> deceived by ambush;
Ancient wall broken through by cannons,
By fire and blood, few received with mercy.

->XXXVIII.<-

The Gallic <note>French</note> people and a foreign nation,
Beyond the mountains, killed, captured, and overthrown;
In the contrary month and near the grape-harvest,
Brought to an agreement by the Lords.

->XXXIX.<-

The seven in three months in concord,
To subjugate the Apennine Alps;
But the storm and the cowardly Ligurian <note>Referring to the inhabitants of Liguria, the coastal region of Italy centered around Genoa.</note>
Overthrow them into sudden ruins.

### CENTURY III.

The great theater will be set up again,
The dice cast, and the nets already spread,
The first will tire too much at the death knell <note>original: "glaz" — a passing bell rung for the dying or dead</note>,
Prostrated by bows already split for a long time.

->XLI.<-

A hunchback will be elected by the council,
A more hideous monster never seen on earth,
The flying blow <note>Likely referring to a projectile or a sudden strike.</note> will pierce his eye,
The traitor received by the King as a faithful man.

->XLII.<-

The child will be born with two teeth in its throat,
Stones will fall like rain in Tuscany <note>original: "Tuscie"</note>,
Shortly after there will be neither wheat nor barley,
To satisfy those who will faint from hunger.

->XLIII.<-

People from around the Tarn, Lot, and Garonne <note>Major rivers in Southwestern France.</note>,
Beware of crossing the Apennine mountains,
Your tomb shall be near Rome and Ancona,
The one with black curly hair will set up a trophy.

->LXIIII.<-
<note>The Roman numeral appears to be a printer's error for XLIV (44), following the sequence of the surrounding quatrains.</note>

When the animal domestic to man,
After great efforts and leaps <note>original: "fauts" — likely from the Old French 'sauts' meaning jumps or bounds.</note> comes to speak,
The lightning flash to the maiden will be so harmful,
Taken from the earth and suspended in the air.

->XLV.<-

The five strangers entered within the Temple,
Their blood will come to profane the earth,
To the people of Toulouse <note>original: "Tholousains"</note> it will be a very hard example,
Of one who will come to abolish their laws.

<header>CENTURY III.</header>

### XLVI.

The sky (of Plancus's city <note>original: "Plencus" — Lucius Munatius Plancus, the Roman founder of the city of Lyon.</note>) presages for us,
By clear signs and by fixed stars,
That the age of its sudden change approaches,
Neither for its good, nor for its evils.

### XLVII.

The old Monarch, driven from his realm,
Will go to seek help in the East:
For fear of the crosses <note>Likely referring to Christian forces or the Crusaders' symbol.</note> he will fold his banner,
To Mytilene <note>original: "Mitylene" — a city on the Greek island of Lesbos.</note> he will go by sea and by land.

### XLVIII.

Seven hundred captives roughly bound,
To murder half, the lot is cast,
The hope nearby will come so promptly,
But not so soon as a fifteenth death <note>May refer to a death occurring on the 15th day of the month or the fifteenth person in a sequence to die.</note>.

### XLIX.

Gallic <note>original: "Gaulois" — referring to France.</note> realm, you will be much changed,
The empire is transferred to a foreign place:
You will be set in other customs and laws,
Rouen <note>original: "Roan"</note> and Chartres <note>original: "Chartes"</note> will do their worst to you.

### L.

The republic of the great city
Will not want to consent to great severity:
The King summoned to come out by the trumpet,
The ladder at the wall, the city shall repent.

### LI.

Paris conspires to commit a great murder,
Blois will cause it to be fully carried out:
Those of Orléans <note>original: "Orleans"</note> will want to reinstate their leader.

<header>CENTURY III.</header>

<meta>Catchword: Saturn</meta>

Before his death he will languish for a long time,
After his death a great marvel will be seen.

### LXXXVII.

French fleet <note>original: "Classe Gauloise." Nostradamus often uses "classe" from the Latin "classis," meaning a fleet.</note>, do not approach Corsica,
Even less Sardinia, or you will repent:
You will all die, frustrated of the help from the Grogne <note>original: "grogne." This is a debated term; it may refer to the "Groyne" (the English name for the port of A Coruña) or perhaps a heraldic reference to a boar.</note>,
Blood will swim; hooded one <note>original: "capus." Possibly referring to a monk or a specific person wearing a cape/hood.</note>, you will not believe me.

### LXXXVIII.

From Barcelona by sea such a great army,
All of Marseille will tremble with fear:
Islands seized, help by sea shut off,
Your betrayer will swim on land.

### LXXXIX.

At that time Cyprus will be frustrated <note>meaning thwarted or disappointed.</note>,
Of its help from those of the Aegean Sea:
Old men slaughtered, but by medlars and lepers <note>original: "mesles & lypres." This phrase is highly obscure; "mesles" may refer to medlar fruit or a "mêlée" (conflict), while "lypres" usually means lepers.</note>
Their King seduced, the Queen even more outraged.

### XC.

The great Satyr and Tiger of Hyrcania <note>Hyrcania was an ancient region located south of the Caspian Sea, in modern-day Iran and Turkmenistan.</note>,
Gift presented to those of the Ocean,
A fleet commander <note>original: "chef de classe."</note> will come out of Carmania <note>An ancient province of the Persian Empire, now in southern Iran.</note>,
Who will take land at the Phocaean Tyrrhenian [port] <note>Refers to Marseille, which was founded by Phocaean Greeks and sits on the Tyrrhenian/Mediterranean coast.</note>.

### XCI.

The tree that had been withered and dead for a long time,
In one night will come to turn green again:
The Saturnian <note>original: "Cron." Likely referring to Chronos/Saturn, implying an aged king.</note> King ill, the Prince with a shackled foot,
Fearing enemies <note>original: "Criant d'ennemis."</note>, he will make the sails bound.

### XCII.

The world near its final period <note>The "last period" refers to the end of an astrological cycle or the end of the world.</note>,

<header>CENTURY III.</header>

Saturn will be late in his return,
The Empire transferred toward the swarthy nations <note>original: "nations Brode." This term is obscure; it may mean "dark-skinned" or refer to "embroidered" (French: *brodé*) garments, suggesting people of the Levant or North Africa.</note>,
The eye torn out at Narbonne by the Goshawk <note>original: "Autour." In Old French, a goshawk, often used as a heraldic symbol or an omen of a predator.</note>.

->XCIII.<-

In Avignon, the entire head of the Empire
Shall be halted because of a desolate Paris:
Tricastin <note>original: "Tricast." Referring to the territory of Saint-Paul-Trois-Châteaux in Provence, once home to the Tricastini people.</note> will hold the Hannibal-like wrath,
Lyon will be poorly consoled by the change:

->XCIV.<-

For five hundred years more, account shall be kept
Of him who was the ornament of his time:
Then suddenly he will give great light,
Which will make those of that century very content.

->XCV.<-

The Moorish law will be seen to fail,
Followed by another much more seductive:
Borysthenes <note>original: "Boristhenes." The ancient Greek name for the Dnieper River, usually referring to the lands of the North, such as Russia or Ukraine.</note> will be the first to give way,
Through gifts and a more attractive tongue.

->XCVI.<-

The Chief of Fossano <note>A town in the Piedmont region of Italy.</note> will have his throat cut
By the leader of the bloodhound and the greyhound:
The deed perpetrated by those of the Tarpeian Rock <note>original: "mont Tarpée." A cliff on the Capitoline Hill in Rome, used in antiquity as an execution site for traitors.</note>,
Saturn in Leo, the 13th of February.

->XCVII.<-

A new law will occupy a new land,
Toward Syria, Judea, and Palestine:
The great Barbarian Empire shall collapse <note>original: "corruer," from the Latin *corruere*, meaning to fall in ruins or decay.</note>,
Before Phoebus <note>original: "Phiebés." A classical name for the Sun or the sun god Apollo; here referring to a solar cycle or century.</note> completes his century.

<header>CENTURY IV</header>

Surprised by night, guards interrupted:
The sentinels <term>excubies: from the Latin *excubiae*, meaning sentinels or guards on watch</term> and watches of Saint-Quentin <note>A town in northern France that was the site of a major Spanish victory over the French in 1557.</note>,
Guards slaughtered and the gates broken.

->IX.<-

The leader of the camp in the midst of the crowd,
Will be wounded in the thighs by a bowshot,
While Geneva, in tears and distress,
Will be betrayed by Lausanne and the Swiss.

->X.<-

The young Prince, falsely accused,
Will throw the camp into trouble and quarrels:
The leader murdered for his support,
To appease the scepter <note>To restore or satisfy royal authority.</note>: then to heal the King's Evil <term>scrofula: a skin disease that French kings were traditionally believed to heal by their touch</term>.

->XI.<-

He who will have covered himself with the great cape <note>Likely referring to the Pope or a high-ranking cleric wearing the *cappa magna*.</note>,
Will be led to commit <note>original: "patrer." Derived from the Latin *patrare*, meaning to perform or perpetrate.</note> some deed:
The twelve red ones <note>The college of Cardinals, known for their red robes.</note> will come to soil the tablecloth,
Under murder, murder will come to be perpetrated.

->XII.<-

The larger camp put to flight and routed.
Hardly much further will it be pursued:
The camp broken up <term>ostrecampé: from the old military term meaning to strike or move a camp</term> and the legion reduced,
Then out of the Gauls <note>France.</note> it will be entirely chased.

->XIII.<-

News reported of a greater loss,
Once the report is made, the camp will be astonished:
United bands revolted against one another,
The double phalanx <term>phalange: an ancient Greek infantry formation of troops standing in close ranks</term> will abandon the great leader.

# CENTURY IV

...double phalanxes <note>Continuing from the previous page: "The double phalanxes [phallanges] will be abandoned."</note>
Sudden death of the first personage <note>A high-ranking leader or monarch.</note>,
Another will be changed and placed in the reign:
Soon, or late come to such high and low age,
That he must be feared by land and sea.

->X V.<-

From where one thinks to bring famine,
From there will come the satiation:
The eye of the sea <note>Likely a strategic port or a maritime observation point.</note> by dog-like greed <term>auare canine: from the Latin *canina avaritia*, implying an insatiable or vicious greed</term>,
For the one, the other will give oil and wheat.

->X V I.<-

The free city <note>Likely a city-state or an autonomous municipality like Geneva.</note> made a servant of liberty,
Made an asylum for the profligate and dreamers:
The King changed, not so arrogant <term>proterue: from the Latin *protervus*, meaning insolent or impudent</term> toward them,
From a hundred they will have become more than a thousand.

->X V I I.<-

Change at Beaune, Nuits, Chalon, and Dijon,
The Duke wanting to improve the Bar region <note>original: "la Barrée". Refers to the territory of Bar-le-Duc or the Barois.</note>
Walking near the river, fish, beak of a diver <note>Referring to a "plongeon," a type of diving water bird.</note>,
He will see the tail: the gate will be barred.

->X V I I I.<-

Those most learned in celestial matters <note>Astrologers or astronomers.</note>,
Will be reproved by ignorant Princes:
Punished by Edict, chased away like criminals <note>original: "scelestes". From the Latin *scelestus*, meaning wicked or villainous.</note>,
And put to death wherever they are found.

->X I X.<-

Before Rouen a siege laid by the Insubrians <note>The Insubres were a Celtic people of northern Italy; here it likely refers to the inhabitants of Milan or Italian mercenaries.</note>,
The passages closed by land and sea:
From Hainaut and Flanders, from Ghent and those of Liège,
Levies <note>The original "Pardons leuées" likely refers to troops raised through payments or official summons.</note> will ravage the shores.

# CENTURY IV

Diminishing the sacred prayers. <note>This line concludes Quatrain XXV from the previous page: "It will cause the earth to tremble, / Diminishing the sacred prayers."</note>

### X X V I.

The great swarm of bees shall arise, <note>Nostradamus writes this quatrain in the Occitan/Provençal dialect of his home region.</note>
That will not know from whence they have come:
Night ambush, the watch beneath the trellises,
City betrayed by five tongues not revealed. <note>original: "non nudos," meaning the languages are hidden or the speakers are disguised.</note>

### X X V I I.

Salon, Mausole, Tarascon of Sextus, the arch, <note>References to towns in Provence: Salon-de-Provence, Saint-Rémy-de-Provence (Mausole), and Tarascon. "Sextus" refers to *Aquae Sextiae*, modern-day Aix-en-Provence.</note>
Where the pyramid still stands upright: <note>Refers to the Roman mausoleum at Glanum, which was called the "pyramid" in the 16th century.</note>

A shameful ransom at the temple of Artemis.

### X X V I I I.

When Venus shall be covered by the Sun,
Beneath the splendor shall be a hidden form:
Mercury in the fire shall have uncovered them,
By warlike noise, he will be put to the insult. <note>An astronomical prophecy; "insult" here implies a military assault or sudden attack.</note>

### X X I X.

The Sun hidden, eclipsed by Mercury,
Will be placed only for the second heaven:
Of Vulcan, Hermes shall be made the pasture, <note>Hermes is the Greek name for Mercury; Vulcan represents fire or the forge.</note>

<margin>4.</margin>
### X X X.

Eleven times more the Moon will not want the Sun,
Both increased and lowered in degree:
And set so low that little gold shall be sewn,
That after famine and plague, the secret is discovered.

# CENTURY IV

Summoned by his disciples to be immortal,
Eyes to the south, in the breast, hands, and body to the fire. <note>This concludes Quatrain XXXI. The imagery suggests a ritual or a martyrdom by fire.</note>

->XXXII.<-

In places and times flesh shall give way to fish,
The common law shall be made to the contrary:
The old man will hold fast, then be removed from the midst,
"All things common among friends" put far behind. <note>original: "Le Panta chiona philon," a French transliteration of the Greek "panta koinà phílōn," a Pythagorean maxim meaning "all things are shared among friends." Nostradamus suggests a breakdown of social or communal order.</note>

->XXXIII.<-

Jupiter joined more to Venus than to the Moon,
Appearing in a white fullness:
Venus hidden beneath the whiteness of Neptune,
Struck by Mars through the heavy branch. <note>This quatrain uses astrological alignments to predict a moment of conflict. Neptune here likely refers to the sea rather than the planet, which was undiscovered in the 16th century.</note>

->XXXIV.<-

The great one led captive from a foreign land,
Bound in gold, offered to King CHIREN: <note>CHIREN is widely considered an anagram for "Henric," referring to King Henry II of France.</note>
Who in Aufonia and Milan will lose the war, <note>Aufonia is a poetic classical name for Italy.</note>
And all his host put to fire and sword. <note>original: "os," an archaic term for "ost," meaning an army or military host.</note>

->XXXV.<-

The fire extinguished, the virgins will betray
The greater part of the new band:
Lightning, sword, and lance alone will protect the King,
Etruscan and Corsican, by night the throat [pierced by] the blade. <note>original: "allumelle," an old term for the blade of a knife or sword. "Etrusque" refers to Tuscany.</note>

->XXXVI.<-

The new games restored in Gaul, <note>Gaul refers to France.</note>
After victories in the Insubrian campaign: <note>Insubria is the historical region of northern Italy surrounding Milan.</note>
Mountains of Hesperia, the great ones bound and trussed, <note>Hesperia is a classical term for the "Western Land," usually referring to Italy or Spain.</note>
Romagna and Spain shall tremble with fear.

->XXXVII.<-

The Gaul will come to penetrate through the high mountains,

# CENTURY IV

...will occupy the great land of the Insubrians, <note>The Insubrians were a Celtic people who lived in what is now Lombardy, Italy; Nostradamus often uses this name to refer to the region around Milan.</note>
Deep within, his army shall enter,
Genoa and Monaco will launch the red fleet. <note>original: "classe rubre," from the Latin *classis* (fleet) and *ruber* (red). This could refer to bloodied ships or ships flying a red flag of war.</note>

->XXXVIII.<-

While the **Duke**, King, and Queen are occupied,
The Byzantine leader will take the captive in Samothrace, <note>Samothrace is a Greek island in the northern Aegean Sea. "Chef Bizant" refers to a leader from Byzantium (modern-day Istanbul/the Ottoman Empire).</note>
Before the assault, they will devour one another,
The iron-shod rebel will follow the trail of blood.

->XXXIX.<-

The people of Rhodes will ask for help,
Abandoned through the neglect of their heirs,
The Arab Empire will reverse its course,
Its cause set right by the Hesperians. <note>original: "Hesperies," a classical term referring to the lands of the West, typically Spain or Italy.</note>

->XL.<-

The fortresses of the besieged, though fortified with iron,
Will be cast down into the abyss by gunpowder,
The traitors will all be shackled alive,
Never did such a pitiful schism befall the church officials.

->XLI.<-

The female sex, held captive as hostages,
Will come by night to deceive the guards,
The commander of the camp, misled by her speech,
Will leave her to the people; it will be a pitiful sight to see.

->XLII.<-

Geneva and Langres by those of Chartres and Dole,
And by Grenoble, captive at Montélimar,
Seyssel and Lausanne, through fraudulent deceit,
Will betray them for sixty marks of gold. <note>A "marc" was a traditional European unit of weight for gold and silver, roughly equivalent to 8 ounces.</note>

# CENTURY IV

### XLIX.

Before the people blood will be shed,
Which will not come from far from high heaven; <note>original: "du haut ciel" - this could imply a celestial omen, like a comet, or a divine judgment.</note>
But for a long time it will not be understood,
The spirit of a single one will come to testify to it.

### L.

Libra <note>The Scales, an astrological sign associated with balance and justice.</note> will see the Westerners <note>original: "les Hesperies," a classical term referring to the lands of the West, typically Italy, Spain, or in later interpretations, the Americas.</note> reign,
Holding the Monarchy of sky and earth;
None shall see the forces of Asia perish,
Until seven hold the hierarchy in order.

### LI.

A greedy Duke following his enemy,
Will enter inside, obstructing the phalanx; <note>A "phalanx" refers to a compact military formation of infantry.</note>
Hasten on foot, they will pursue so closely,
That the day of conflict is near the Ganges. <note>The sacred river of India, here perhaps representing a distant eastern boundary or a global conflict.</note>

### LII.

In a city besieged, men and women at the walls,
Enemies outside, the leader ready to surrender;
The wind will be strong against the soldiers,
They will be driven away by lime, dust, and ash. <note>This describes an early form of chemical or environmental warfare where the wind is used to blind or choke the attackers.</note>

### LIII.

The fugitives and exiles recalled,
Fathers and sons filling the deep wells;
The cruel father and his kin suffocated,
His even worse son submerged in the well.

### LIIII.

Of the name that never belonged to a Gallic <note>French</note> King,
Never was there a lightning bolt so feared,
Italy, Spain, and the English trembling,

# CENTURY IV

Toward a foreign woman, greatly attentive, <note>This line completes the final quatrain from the previous page (LIIII).</note>

->LV.<-

When the crow upon the rooftop of joined brick
For seven hours does nothing but cry out:
Death foretold by a statue stained with blood,
A tyrant murdered, the people praying to the Gods. <note>In the 16th century, animal behavior and "weeping" or "bleeding" statues were commonly interpreted as portents of a ruler's death.</note>

->LVI.<-

After a victory won by a rabid tongue,
The spirit tempted in tranquility and rest,
the bloody victor makes a speech following the conflict,
Roasting the tongue, the flesh, and the bones. <note>original: "rabieuse langue" - this could refer to a victory won through oratory or propaganda rather than just arms, followed by a brutal execution.</note>

->LVII.<-

Ignorant envy directed at the great King,
He will propose to forbid the writings:
His wife, no longer a wife, tempted by another,
No more "double two," neither strength nor cries. <note>original: "Plus double deux" - likely a cryptic reference to the number four, perhaps indicating a date or a group of four people.</note>

->LVIII.<-

The burning sun stuck in the throat,
With human blood to water the Etruscan land: <note>Etruria is the ancient name for Tuscany, Italy.</note>
The leader, bucket of water in hand, leads his son to flee,
A captive lady led to Turkish lands.

->LIX.<-

Two besieged in burning fervor,
Quenched of life by thirst for two full cups,
The fortress worn away, and a dreaming old man,
To the Genevans, Nira showed the track. <note>Nira is often considered an anagram by scholars, possibly for "Arin" or "Noir" (Black), or a reference to a specific historical figure unknown to us today.</note>

->LX.<-

The seven children left as hostages,
The third will come to slaughter his child, <note>original: "trucider" - a word implying a particularly brutal or violent murder.</note>

# CENTURY IV

Two will be pierced through by the sword by his son,
Genoa and Florence will come to hide them. <note>original: "enconder" - an archaic term likely meaning to hide, bury, or enclose.</note>

->L X I.<-

The old man mocked and deprived of his place,
By the stranger who will suborn him,
But [the actions] of his son eaten before his face, <note>This could be a metaphor for the son consuming the father's legacy or a literal reference to a gruesome act common in tragic literature.</note>
The brother at Chartres, Orléans, and Rouen will betray.

->L X I I.<-

A colonel plots with ambition,
He will seize the greatest army,
Against his prince, a feigned deception,
And he will be discovered under his leafy branches. <note>original: "soubs sa ramée" - likely an idiom suggesting the plotter is caught in the very trap or "covert" he built for himself.</note>

->L X I I I.<-

The Celtic army against the mountaineers,
Who will be spotted and caught in the trap: <note>original: "à la pipée" - a term from bird-hunting where a whistle or decoy is used to lure prey.</note>
Fresh peasants will soon drive back the bloodthirsty ones,
All cast down by the edge of the sword.

->L X I I I I.<-

The failing one in the clothes of a commoner,
Will come to tempt the King with his offense:
Fifteen bolts, <note>original: "fouldartz" - possibly "foul-dards" (deadly darts/arrows) or "foudroyés" (those struck by lightning).</note> for the most part outcasts, <note>original: "Vstageois" - an obscure term that may refer to a specific group of rebels or inhabitants of a particular region like Ustaritz.</note>
The end of his life and the head of his fortune.

->L X V.<-

To the deserter of the great fortress,
After he has abandoned his post,
His adversary will display such great prowess,
The Emperor, soon dead, will be condemned.

->L X V I.<-

Under the feigned appearance of seven shaved heads <note>A "shaved head" (tonsure) was the traditional mark of a monk or cleric in the 16th century.</note>

# CENTURY IV

Various scouts will be scattered,
Wells and fountains sprinkled with poisons,
At the fort of Genoa, devourers of men. <note>This likely refers to a brutal siege or famine where the inhabitants are driven to extremes.</note>

### LXVII.

The year that Saturn and Mars are equally "combust," <note>original: "combust" - an astrological term for a planet that is so close to the sun that its influence is "burnt up" or hidden from view.</note>
The air very parched, a long trajectory, <note>This could describe a comet or a meteor passing through the sky.</note>
By secret fires a great place parched with heat,
Little rain, hot wind, wars, raids.

### LXVIII.

In the year very near, not far from Venus,
The two greatest of Asia and Africa,
From the Rhine and Danube <note>original: "Hister," the ancient Latin name for the lower Danube River.</note> they will be said to have come,
Cries, tears at Malta and the Ligurian coast.

### LXIX.

The great city will be held by exiles,
The citizens dead, murdered, and driven out,
Those of Aquileia will promise to Parma
To show the entrance through unmapped places.

### LXX.

Very near the great Pyrenees mountains,
One shall direct a great troop against the Eagle: <note>The "Eagle" traditionally symbolized the Holy Roman Empire or the Habsburg dynasty.</note>
Veins opened, forces exterminated,
The leader will come to chase them as far as Pau.

### LXXI.

In place of a bride, the daughters slaughtered,
Murder by great error, no one will survive: <note>original: "superstile" - an archaic term for surviving or a survivor.</note>
Within the well, the drowned women fully clothed,
The bride extinguished by a draught of Aconite. <note>original: "Aconile" - Aconite (Monkshood) is a highly toxic plant used as a poison since antiquity.</note>

### LXXII.

# CENTURY IV

The people of Agenois by Agen and Lectoure, <note>original: "Artomiques" and "L'Estore." The Artomiques likely refer to the Nitiobroges, a Celtic tribe from the region of Agen.</note>
At Saint-Felix they will hold their assembly:
Those of Bazas will come at an unlucky hour,
To seize Condom and Marsan promptly.

->LXXIII.<-

The great nephew will prove by force,
The sin committed by a faint heart,
The Duke will put Ferrara and Asti to the test,
At the time when the pantomime takes place in the evening. <note>A "pantomime" refers to a theatrical performance or a masked play, suggesting a moment of distraction or celebration.</note>

->LXXIIII.<-

At Lake Geneva and those of the Brannovices, <note>original: "lac Lyman" and "Brannonices." The Brannovices were an ancient Gallic tribe located near modern-day Burgundy.</note>
All assembled against those of Aquitaine,
Many Germans, and even more Swiss,
Will be defeated along with those of Maine. <note>original: "humaine." Interpreted by many scholars as a reference to the region of Maine or "the human ones," but in the context of geography, Maine fits the military alignment.</note>

->LXXV.<-

Ready to fight, there will be a desertion,
The opposing leader will obtain the victory:
The rear guard will provide the defense,
Those who fail dying in the white territory. <note>This "white territory" likely refers to the snow-covered Alps or perhaps a region marked by white heraldry.</note>

->LXXVI.<-

The people of Agen will be harassed by those of Périgord, <note>original: "Nictobriges," another reference to the Nitiobroges tribe.</note>
Holding ground as far as the Rhône,
The associate of Gascons and Bigorre, <note>original: "Begorne."</note>
Will betray the temple, while the priest is at the pulpit. <note>original: "prosne" — the part of the mass where the priest addresses the congregation with announcements or a sermon.</note>

->LXXVII.<-

Selin the Monarch, Italy made peaceful, <note>The name "Selin" is frequently used by Nostradamus to refer to a "Crescent" monarch (from the Greek *selene* for moon), often associated with the Ottoman Empire or a future great Christian king.</note>
Realms united, Christian King of the world,
Dying, he will wish to lie in the land of Blois, <note>original: "blesique."</note>
After having chased the pirates from the sea.

# CENTURY IV

### LXXVIII.

The great army of the civil struggle, <note>original: "pugne," from the Latin "pugna," meaning battle or fight.</note>
At night, Parma is found by the stranger,
Seventy-nine murdered within the city,
The foreigners all put to the sword.

### LXXIX.

Royal blood flee! Monheurt, Marsan, Aiguillon, <note>These are towns in the Gascony region of southwest France.</note>
The people of Bordeaux and the Landes will be filled,
Navarre and Bigorre, points and spurs,
Deep with hunger, they will devour cork-oak acorns. <note>original: "Liege glandes." This likely refers to "chêne-liège" (cork-oak). Eating acorns was a traditional sign of extreme famine.</note>

### LXXX.

Near the great river, a great trench, excavated earth,
The water will be divided into fifteen parts.
The city taken; fire, blood, cries, and conflict break out,
And the greater part concerns the Coliseum. <note>This likely refers to Rome, given the mention of the Coliseum and the engineering of a river, possibly the Tiber.</note>

### LXXXI.

He will promptly build a pontoon of small boats,
To pass the army of the great Belgian Prince:
Into the depths, and not far from Brussels,
Overcome and cut down, seven by the pike.

### LXXXII.

A mass of people approaches, coming from Slavonia, <note>original: "Esclauonie." A region in modern-day Croatia, often used to refer to the Balkan territories.</note>
The Old Destroyer will ruin the city: <note>original: "L'Olestant." This is a cryptic term, possibly derived from the Greek "ollymi," meaning to destroy.</note>
He will see his Romagna quite desolate, <note>original: "Romainie." Refers to the Romagna region of Italy.</note>
Then he will not know how to extinguish the great flame.

### LXXXIII.

In a night combat, the valiant captain,
Defeated, will flee with few people ruined:
His people stirred up, a sedition not in vain,

# CENTURY IV

His own son will keep him afflicted.

### LXXXIV.

A great man of Auxerre <note>A city in Burgundy, France.</note> will die very miserably,
Driven out by those who were under his command
Bound with chains, then with a coarse rope,
In the year that Mars, Venus, and the Sun are positioned in summer.

### LXXXV.

The white coal shall be driven out by the black,
Made a prisoner and led to the tumbrel: <note>A two-wheeled cart, often associated with carrying prisoners to their execution.</note>
The Moorish Camel with legs entwined,
Then the younger son will lure the hobby-hawk. <note>original: "aubereau," referring to a small species of hawk.</note>

### LXXXVI.

The year that Saturn is conjoined in a water sign, <note>original: "en eau," likely referring to the zodiac sign Aquarius or the element of water.</note>
Along with the Sun, the strong and powerful King:
At Reims and Aix he will be received and anointed, <note>Reims was the traditional coronation site for French Kings; Aix (Aachen) was the site for Holy Roman Emperors.</note>
After his conquests, he will murder the innocent.

### LXXXVII.

A son of a King learned in so many languages,
Differing from his elder in his manner of ruling,
His father-in-law, included with the Greatest son,
Will cause a principal supporter to perish.

### LXXXVIII.

The great Anthony, whose name and deeds are sordid,
Consumed by Phthyriase <term>Phthyriase: A horrific medical condition described by ancient and Renaissance authors where the body is allegedly consumed by a parasitic infestation of lice.</term> at his end:
One who will be greedy for lead,
Passing the port of the elected one, he will be plunged.

### LXXXIX.

Thirty from London will secretly conspire,
Against their King, the enterprise on the bridge:

# CENTURY IV

Fate will make him loathe death,
A King, fair-haired and elected, native of Frisia. <note>original: "Frize," referring to Friesland, a coastal region in the Netherlands and Germany.</note>

->XC.<-

The two armies <note>original: "copies," from the Latin *copia*, meaning forces or troops.</note> will not be able to join at the walls,
In that moment, Milan and Ticino <note>A river and region in Lombardy, Northern Italy.</note> will tremble:
Hunger, thirst, and doubt will sting them so strongly,
They will not have a single morsel <note>original: "boucin," likely a bite or mouthful.</note> of meat, bread, or provisions.

->XCI.<-

To the French Duke forced to fight in a duel,
The ship *Melle* will not approach Monaco; <note>original: "Monech."</note>
Wrongly accused, sentenced to perpetual prison,
His son will strive to reign before his death.

->XCII.<-

The severed head of the valiant captain
Will be thrown before his adversary.
His body hung from the yardarm of the fleet, <note>original: "de la claſſe à l'antenne." Nostradamus uses the Latin *classis* for fleet and *antenna* for the spar of a ship's mast.</note>
In confusion, he will flee by oars against a headwind.

->XCIII.<-

A serpent seen near the Royal bed
Will be noticed by a lady at night; the dogs will not bark:
Then a Prince so Royal will be born in France,
Whom all the Princes will see as having come from heaven.

->XCIIII.<-

Two great brothers will be driven out of Spain,
The elder defeated beneath the Pyrenees mountains:
The sea and the Rhône will redden, the blood of Lake Geneva <note>original: "Leman."</note> from Germany,
Narbonne, Béziers, and Agde <note>Towns in the Languedoc region of Southern France; "Blyterre" is an old name for Béziers.</note> contaminated.

->XCV.<-

The kingdom left to two, they will hold it for only a short time,

# CENTURY V

Three years and seven months having passed, they will wage war;
The two vestals <note>Used here to refer to consecrated virgins or perhaps nuns.</note> will rebel against them,
Victor, the younger son, in the land of Armorica <note>original: "Armonique terre"; the ancient name for Brittany in northwestern France.</note>.

->XCVI.<-

The eldest sister of the British Isle
Will be born fifteen years before her brother:
Through her promise, by means of truth-telling <note>original: "verrifique," meaning truth-making or verifying.</note>,
She will succeed to the kingdom of the scales <note>Likely referring to the zodiac sign of Libra, symbolizing justice or a balanced reign.</note>.

->XCVII.<-

The year that Mercury, Mars, and Venus are retrograde,
The lineage of the great Monarch shall not fail:
Elected by the people, frequenting near Gagdole <note>This place name "Gagdole" is obscure; it may be a corruption of a local place name or a Latin term.</note>,
He will grow very old in peace and his reign.

->XCVIII.<-

Those from Albania <note>original: "Albanois"; possibly referring to the people of Alba Longa or a specific military group.</note> will pass into Rome,
By way of Langres, dressed in double-pleated garments <note>original: "demipler affublés"; this may refer to a specific style of military uniform or a corruption of "half-full."</note>,
Marquis and Duke will not pardon any man,
Fire, blood, smallpox <note>original: "morbilles," an old term for measles or eruptive diseases.</note>, no water, and the grain shall fail.

->XCIX.<-

The valiant eldest son of the King’s daughter
Will push the Celts <note>original: "Celtiques."</note> back so deep,
That he will strike with thunderbolts; in such a state <note>original: "arroy," meaning military array or arrangement.</note>
Few and far, then deep into the Hesperians <note>original: "Helperiques," a reference to Hesperia, a poetic name for the "Western" lands of Italy or Spain.</note>.

->C.<-

By celestial fire at the Royal building,
When the light of Mars shall fail:
Seven months of great war, death to people of evil deeds:
Rouen and Évreux will not fail the King.

<summary>This final page of the fifth Century features quatrains concerning royal successions in Britain and France, military incursions into Rome, and celestial omens affecting French cities like Rouen and Évreux.</summary>
<keywords>Brittany, British Isle, Libra, Retrograde, Rome, Langres, Hesperia, Rouen, Évreux, succession</keywords>

# PROPHECIES OF
## Mr. Nostradamus.

### CENTURY V

I.
Before the coming of Celtic <note>Nostradamus frequently uses "Celtic" to refer to the French people or their territories.</note> ruin,
Inside the Temple <note>This may refer to the Temple building in Paris, originally the fortress of the Knights Templar.</note> two will parley:
A dagger to the heart of one mounted on a steed, & a pike,
Without making a sound, they will bury the great man.

->II.<-
Seven conspirators at the banquet will make [their blades] shine,
Against the three, the iron [sword] from the ship:
One will lead the two fleets <note>original: "classes"; from the Latin *classis*, meaning a fleet or naval force.</note> to the great leader,
When by the mallet <note>original: "mail"; possibly referring to a heavy blow or a specific tool.</note>. A Denier <note>A small silver coin used in medieval and early modern France.</note> strikes him on the forehead.

->III.<-
The successor to the Duchy will come,
Much further than the Tuscan sea:
A Gallic <note>French</note> branch will hold Florence,
In its lap, the nautical Frog <note>original: "Rane"; from the Latin *rana*. This is often interpreted as a derogatory term for a person or a symbolic reference to a marshy region.</note> of agreement.

->IV.<-
The large mastiff <note>original: "mastin"; a large, powerful breed of dog often used as a metaphor for a fierce guard or leader.</note> driven out of the city,
Will be vexed by the strange alliance,
After having chased the stag through the fields,
The wolf and the Bear will distrust one another.

->V.<-
Under the holy shadow of removing from servitude,

<keywords>Celtic ruin, Temple, conspirators, Tuscany, Florence, mastiff, Gallic branch, prophecy</keywords>

<header>CENTURY V.</header>

<note>The left edge of this page is slightly damaged, but the text has been reconstructed based on known editions of the Prophecies.</note>

> [The pe]ople & city will usurp it themselves:
> [Wo]rse it will be through the frauds of a young whore,
> [De]livered to the field reading the false prologue <note>original: "proëme"; a preface or introductory discourse.</note>.

### VI.

> The Augur <note>In ancient Rome, an Augur was a priest who interpreted the will of the gods from the flight of birds.</note> placing his hand on the King's head,
> Will come to pray for the peace of Italy:
> In the left hand he will come to change the scepter,
> From King he will become a peaceful Emperor.

### VII.

> The bones of the Triumvir <note>A member of a "Triumvirate," a group of three powerful leaders, often associated with the fall of the Roman Republic.</note> will be found,
> Seeking deep enigmatic treasure:
> Those in the surrounding area will not be at rest,
> From hollowing out marble and metallic lead.

### VIII.

> The living fire will be left, death hidden,
> Inside the globes, horrible and terrifying,
> By night, released as dust upon the fleet <note>original: "classe"; from the Latin *classis*, meaning a naval fleet.</note> and city,
> The city on fire, the enemy in a favorable position.

### IX.

> The great arch brought down to its foundations,
> The friend anticipated by the captive leader:
> Born of a lady: a forehead, a hairy face <note>This may refer to a "monstrous birth," which in the 16th century was often interpreted as an omen of political upheaval.</note>,
> Then by cunning, the Duke trapped to his death.

### X.

> A Celtic <note>As noted previously, Nostradamus uses "Celtic" to refer to the French.</note> leader wounded in the conflict
> Near a cellar seeing his men struck down dead:
> Pressed by blood, wounds, and enemies,
> And rescued by four unknown men.

<header>CENTURY V.</header>

The sea will be safely crossed by those of the Sun,
Those of Venus <note>Likely referring to the followers of Islam or a specific Mediterranean power; Venus was sometimes associated with the East in astrological geography.</note> will hold all of Africa:
Saturn will no longer occupy their realm,
And the Asian part will change.

->XII.<-

Near Lake Geneva <note>original: "lac Leman"</note> it will be led,
By a strange girl wanting to betray the city,
Before her murder at Augsburg, the great following,
And those of the Rhine will come to invade it.

->XIII.<-

With great fury the Roman King <note>Often referring to the Holy Roman Emperor.</note> will want
To vex Belgium with a barbarian phalanx:
Gnashing fury will drive out the Libyan people
From Pannonia <note>An ancient province of the Roman Empire, covering parts of modern-day Hungary, Austria, and the Balkans.</note> all the way to the Pillars of Hercules <note>original: "Hercules la hare"; the Strait of Gibraltar.</note>.

->XIIII.<-

Saturn and Mars in Leo, Spain captive,
Caught in the conflict by a Libyan leader,
Near Malta, Djerba <note>original: "Heredde"; likely referring to the island of Djerba off the coast of Tunisia, a site of major naval battles.</note> taken alive,
And the Roman scepter will be struck by the Cock <note>The "Coq" is a traditional symbol of France.</note>.

->XV.<-

While sailing, the great Pontiff <note>The Pope.</note> taken captive,
Afterward, the clergy will fail in great turmoil:
The second one elected being absent, his wealth is divided,
His favorite bastard put to death.

->XVI.<-

At its high price, the Sabaean tear <note>original: "lerme sabée"; refers to frankincense, an aromatic resin from the Land of Sheba used in religious and funerary rites.</note> no more,
To turn human flesh into ash through death,
At the Isle of Pharos <note>The site of the great lighthouse of Alexandria, Egypt.</note> disturbed by Crusaders,
When a harsh specter shall appear at Rhodes.

->CENTURY V<-

### XVII.

At night, as the King passes near a narrow alleyway <note>original: "Andronne"; a narrow passage or side street.</note>,
The one from Cyprus and the principal guard <note>original: "guetre"; likely a variant of "guet" (the watch) or referring to military gear.</note>:
The King fails, the hand flees along the Rhône,
The conspirators will go to put him to death.

### XVIII.

From grief, the unhappy, ruined man <note>original: "l’infelix profligé"; from the Latin *infelix* (unhappy) and *profligatus* (wretched or ruined).</note> will die,
The victor <note>original: "vitrix"; Latin for a female victor, often used here to denote the winning side or "Victory" personified.</note> will celebrate a great sacrifice <note>original: "hecatombe"; historically, a Greek sacrifice of one hundred oxen.</note>:
Ancient law and a French edict drawn up,
The wall and the seventh Prince fall to the tomb on the same day <note>original: "ibur"; likely a printing error for "jour" (day).</note>.

### XIX.

The great Royal treasury of gold and bronze increased,
The peace <note>original: "pache"; a Provençal form of "pacte" (pact/peace).</note> broken by a youth in open war:
A people afflicted by a lamented leader,
The earth will be covered in barbarian blood.

### XX.

From there, a great army will cross the Alps,
A little before, a wretched monster <note>original: "vapin"; an obscure term often interpreted as a scoundrel or a specific geographic reference.</note> will be born:
Prodigious and sudden, it will turn,
The great Tuscan to his nearest <note>original: "propin"; from the Latin *propinquus* (nearby).</note> place.

### XXI.

By the death of the Latin Monarch <note>Typically referring to the Pope or a powerful Catholic king like the King of France.</note>,
Those whom he helped throughout his reign,
The fire will shine, the booty will be divided,
Public death for the bold who ran into it <note>original: "incoruz"; from the French "encouru" (incurred/ran into).</note>.

### XXII.

Before the Great One in Rome has given up his soul,
Great terror for the foreign army:
An ambush by squadrons near Parma,

# CENTURY V.

Then the two reds will make good cheer together. <note>This is the final line of Quatrain XXII, completing the thought from the previous page regarding a shared celebration or alliance between two "red" figures, often interpreted as Cardinals or revolutionaries.</note>

->XXIII.<-

The two contented ones will be joined together,
When the majority are united with Mars <note>Mars refers to the planet of war or the god of war itself, suggesting a time of military mobilization.</note>:
The great man of Africa trembles in fear,
The **DUUMVIRATE** <note>A term for a leadership shared by two people, originating from Roman political offices.</note> is pulled apart by the chase.

->XXIV.<-

The kingdom and law raised under Venus <note>Venus represents peace, love, or a specific feminine influence.</note>,
Saturn will have empire over Jupiter:
The law and kingdom raised by the Sun,
Will endure the worst at the hands of those of Saturn <note>In astrology, Saturn often represents hardship, restriction, or old age, whereas Jupiter represents expansion and authority.</note>.

->XXV.<-

The Arab Prince—Mars, Sun, Venus, and Leo—
The kingdom of the Church will succumb by sea:
Toward Persia, nearly a million [men],
The true serpent <note>original: "ver. serp."; likely an abbreviation for the Latin *verus serpens*.</note> will invade Byzantium and Egypt.

->XXVI.<-

The Slavic people <note>original: "la gent esclaue"; this can mean "enslaved people," but in the 16th century, it specifically referred to the Slavic nations.</note> by a stroke of military luck,
Will be raised to such a high degree:
They will change their prince, one from the provinces will be born,
The army <note>original: "copie"; from the Latin *copia*, meaning military forces or troops.</note> raised in the mountains will cross the sea.

->XXVII.<-

By fire and arms not far from the Black Sea <note>original: "mar negro".</note>,
One will come from Persia to occupy Trebizond <note>A historical city on the Black Sea coast of modern-day Turkey.</note>:
Pharos <note>original: "Phato"; likely referring to the Pharos of Alexandria or the island itself.</note> and Mytilene <note>original: "Methelin"; an island in the Aegean Sea.</note> tremble, the sun is joyful <note>original: "sol alegro".</note>,
The waves of the Adriatic covered in Arab blood.

->XXVIII.<-

The arm hanging and the leg bound,
Face pale, a dagger hidden in the breast;

<header>CENTURY V.</header>

...kings who will take oaths in the midst of the fray,
At the great man of Genoa, the blade will be released. <note>original: "fer latché." This likely refers to a sword or dagger being drawn or used in an assassination or battle.</note>

### XXIX.

Liberty will not be recovered,
A dark, proud, base, and unjust man will occupy it,
When the matter of the bridge is completed,
The republic of Venice will be angered by Hister. <note>Hister: The classical Latin name for the lower Danube River, frequently used by Nostradamus to indicate a geographical region or, as many modern interpreters suggest, a cryptic reference to a specific 20th-century dictator.</note>

### XXX.

All around the great city,
Soldiers will be lodged in fields and towns:
To launch the assault on Paris, Rome incited,
On the bridge then, a great looting will be carried out.

### XXXI.

Through the land of Attica, the source of wisdom, <note>Attica: The region of Greece encompassing Athens, used here to represent Greek intellectual heritage.</note>
Which is currently the rose of the world:
The bridge ruined, and its great preeminence,
Will be subjected and shipwrecked by the waves.

### XXXII.

Where everything is good, everything well, Sun and Moon
Are abundant, its ruin approaches:
From Heaven, it advances to winnow your fortune,
In the same state as the seventh rock. <note>The "seventh rock" may refer to a specific astrological configuration or a legendary topographical feature.</note>

### XXXIII.

Of the leading men of the rebelled city,
Who will hold firm to regain their liberty:
To cut down the males in an unhappy fray,
Cries and screams at Nantes, a piteous sight to see. <note>Nantes: A major city in western France; this quatrain foretells a violent suppression of a revolt there.</note>

### XXXIIII.

From the deepest part of the English West, <note>original: "l'Occident Anglois." This could refer to the British Isles or English territories in the Americas.</note>

<meta>This page continues the fifth Century of Nostradamus's prophecies, following the themes of naval movements and political betrayal established on page 45. <header>CENTURY V.</header>

The Royal blood will be so very mixed,
The Gauls <note>Referring to the French people.</note> of Hesperia <term>Hesperia: A classical name meaning "the Western land," used by ancient writers to refer to either Italy or Spain.</term> will be constrained:
One will wait until the term has run out,
And until the memory of the voice has perished.

->X L I.<-

Night under the shadows and a nocturnal day,
Ser. I <note>Often interpreted as a cryptic name or abbreviation for a ruler.</note> in reign and sovereign goodness:
He will cause his blood to be reborn from the ancient urn,
Renewing the Golden Age <note>In classical mythology, the Golden Age was a period of primordial peace and prosperity.</note> in place of the age of brass.

->X L I I.<-

Mars <note>The Roman god of war, representing military conflict.</note> elevated to his highest belfry,
Will make the Allobrogians <term>Allobrox: An ancient Celtic tribe of the Savoy region in the Alps.</term> withdraw from France:
The Lombard people <note>Inhabitants of Lombardy, Northern Italy.</note> will cause such great terror,
To those of the Eagle <note>The Eagle typically symbolizes the Holy Roman Empire.</note> included under the Balance <note>The zodiac sign Libra, symbolizing justice or a specific diplomatic alignment.</note>.

->X L I I I.<-

The great ruin of the sacred ones is not far off,
Provence, Naples, Sicily, Séez, and Pons:
In Germany, at the Rhine and Cologne,
Vexed to death by all those of Mainz <term>Magonce: The Latin name for the German city of Mainz.</term>.

->X L I I I I.<-

By sea the red one <note>Likely referring to a Cardinal, known for their red vestments.</note> will be taken by pirates,
The peace will be troubled because of him:
Anger and greed will commit through a "holy act,"
To the great Pontiff <note>The Pope.</note> the army will be doubled.

->X L V.<-

The great Empire will soon be desolated,
And transferred near the Ardennes forest <term>Arduenne silue: The Latin name for the Ardennes forest, spanning parts of Belgium, Luxembourg, and France.</term>:
The two bastards beheaded by the eldest,
And Ahenobarbus <term>Ænodarb: From the Latin 'Ahenobarbus' (Bronze-beard), the family name of the Emperor Nero; used here to signify a cruel or tyrannical ruler.</term> will reign, born of the kite <note>A "milue" or kite is a bird of prey, often used as a metaphor for a predatory nature.</note>.

<meta>This page continues the fifth Century of Nostradamus's prophecies. The previous page concluded with descriptions of naval conflict and the influence of a "red" figure (likely a Cardinal). This page shifts focus toward papal elections, the threat of Ottoman ("Arab") expansion into Europe, and the shifting alliances of Eastern and Northern European powers.</meta>

<header>CENTURY V.</header>

### XLVI.
Through red hats <note>A common metonym for Cardinals of the Catholic Church, referring to their distinctive headwear.</note> quarrels and new ones
When the Sabine <note>Likely referring to a Pope of Italian or Sabine descent.</note> will have been elected: <gloss>schism</gloss>
Great sophisms <note>Fallacious or deceptive arguments.</note> will be produced against him,
Rome will be injured by those of Alba <term>Albanois: Inhabitants of Alba Longa or the Alban Hills near Rome, though sometimes interpreted as Albanians.</term>.

### XLVII.
The great Arab will march far forward,
But he will be betrayed by the Byzantines <note>Referring to the inhabitants of Istanbul/Constantinople, the seat of the Ottoman Empire.</note>:
Ancient Rhodes will come to meet him,
And greater evil will come through the other Pannonian <term>Pannonois: People of Pannonia, an ancient Roman province covering parts of modern-day Hungary, Austria, and the Balkans.</term>.

### XLVIII.
After the great affliction of the scepter <note>A reference to the suffering or downfall of a monarchy.</note>,
Two enemies will be defeated by them:
Fleets from Africa will be born to the Pannonians,
By sea and land they will do horrible deeds.

### XLIX.
Not one from Spain, but from ancient France,
Will be elected for the trembling vessel <note>The "Bark of Saint Peter," a traditional metaphor for the Papacy and the Catholic Church.</note>,
A promise will be made to the enemy,
Who during his reign will be a cruel plague.

### L.
The year that the brothers of the lily <note>The fleur-de-lis, the heraldic symbol of the French Royal house.</note> come of age,
One of them will hold the great Romania <note>The Romagna region of Italy, then part of the Papal States.</note>:
The mountains tremble, the Latin passage is opened,
Agreement <note>Original: "Pache," likely from the Latin "pax" (peace) or "pactum" (pact).</note>: to march against the fort of Armenia.

### L. <note>This quatrain is misnumbered in the original text as a second "L" (50) instead of "LI" (51).</note>
The people of Dacia <term>Dace: An ancient region encompassing modern Romania and Moldova.</term>, England, and Poland,
And Bohemia will form a new league:
To pass beyond the pillars of Hercules <note>The Strait of Gibraltar, considered the edge of the known world in antiquity.</note>,

<meta>This page continues Century V of Nostradamus's prophecies. <header>CENTURY V.</header>

...ancient ones, Tyrants to prepare a cruel intrigue.

---

->LII.<-

He will be a King who will offer the opposite,
Exiles raised up over the kingdom:
The chaste Hippolytan people <note>Likely an allusion to the mythological Hippolytus, often interpreted here as a symbol of purity or a specific religious order.</note> will swim in blood,
And it will flourish for a long time under such a banner.

---

->LIII.<-

The law of the Sun <note>original: "Sol"</note> and Venus contending,
Appropriating the spirit of prophecy:
Neither the one nor the other will be understood,
Through the Sun will be held the law of the great Messiah.

---

->LIV.<-

From the Euxine Sea <term>Euxine: The ancient name for the Black Sea.</term> and Great Tartary <note>A historical term used by Europeans for the vast region of North and Central Asia from the Caspian Sea to the Pacific Ocean.</note>,
A King will arise who will come to see Gaul <note>The Latin and archaic name for France.</note>;
He will pierce through Alania <note>An Iranian kingdom in the Caucasus, modern-day North Ossetia.</note> and Armenia,
And in Byzantium <note>Istanbul/Constantinople.</note> he will leave a bloody rod <note>original: "Gaule," which is a pun in the original French between the word for a rod or staff and the name for France.</note>.

---

->LV.<-

From the region of Arabia Felix <term>Arabia Felix: "Happy Arabia," the ancient name for the fertile southern Arabian Peninsula, primarily modern-day Yemen.</term>,
One powerful in the Muhammadan law will be born,
To vex Spain and conquer Granada,
And more by sea against the Ligurian <term>Liguſtique: People from Liguria, the coastal region of Italy centered around Genoa.</term> people.

---

->LVI.<-

By the death of the very aged Pontiff,
A Roman of good age will be elected:
It will be said of him that he weakens the see <note>The "Holy See," referring to the jurisdiction and office of the Pope.</note>,
And he will hold it long, and with stinging labor.

---

->LVII.<-

->LVIII.<-

->LIX.<-

When in Artois the bearded star <term>eſtoille en barbe: A traditional term for a comet, which was historically viewed as a portent of doom or significant change.</term> shall fail.

->LX.<-

By a shaven head <note>original: "teſte raſe," a reference to the tonsure of a monk or member of the clergy.</note> a very poor choice shall be made,

So great a fury and rage shall be spoken,

->LXI.<-

<header>CENTURY V</header>

No longer will the great one be in a false sleep,
Anxiety will come to take its rest:
To raise a phalanx of gold, azure, and vermilion <note>These are heraldic colors; "azure and gold" often signified the French monarchy.</note>,
To subjugate Africa and gnaw it to the bone.

->LXX.<-

From the regions subject to the Balance <term>Balance: The zodiac sign Libra, which astrologers of the time associated with specific regions such as Austria, Savoy, or parts of Italy.</term>,
The mountains will be troubled by a great war,
Captives of every sex and all of Byzantium <term>Bisance: Constantinople, the capital of the Ottoman Empire.</term>,
So that at dawn they will cry, "Land to land!" <note>Likely a naval cry upon sighting land or an order to move toward the shore.</note>

->LXXI.<-

By the fury of one who will wait for the water,
By the great rage the entire army <term>exercite: An archaic term derived from the Latin "exercitus," meaning a trained military force.</term> is moved:
Loaded with nobles in seventeen boats,
Along the Rhône <term>Rosne: The Rhône river in France, a strategic corridor for military movement.</term>, a messenger arrived late.

->LXXII.<-

For the pleasure of [the eye] <note>original: "ed," likely a typographical error for "d'œil" (of the eye) or "edict."</note> and the voluptuous,
Poison will be mixed into the Law:
Venus will be in a course so virtuous,
That she will obscure every standard of the Sun <note>original: "a loy," likely "aloi," referring to the purity or standard of gold or currency. This implies a corruption where "Venus" (desire or a specific woman) overshadows the "Sun" (the King or reason).</note>.

->LXXIII.<-

The Church of God shall be persecuted,
And the holy temples shall be plundered,
The child shall strip the mother naked to her shift <note>A "chemise" or shift was the basic undergarment; this suggests total destitution or a shocking reversal of the natural order.</note>,
Arabs shall be allied with the Poles <term>Polons: The people of Poland.</term>.

->LXXIIII.<-

From Trojan blood <note>The "Frankish-Trojan" myth claimed that the French kings were descended from Francus, a survivor of Troy.</note> shall be born a Germanic heart,
Who shall rise to such high power:
He shall drive out the foreign Arabic people,
Returning the Church to its pristine preeminence.

<header>CENTURY V</header>

->LXXV.<-

He will rise high over the property more to the right <note>original: "dextre." In heraldry and augury, the right side was considered favorable or lucky.</note>,
He will not die seated upon the square stone:
Toward the South placed on the left <note>original: "ſeneſtre." The left side was often considered an ill omen (sinister).</note>,
Crooked staff in hand, mouth tightly shut <note>The "crooked staff" likely refers to a bishop’s crozier, suggesting a silent or secretive religious leader.</note>.

->LXXVI.<-

In a free place he will pitch his pavilion,
And will not wish to take a place in the cities:
Aix, Carpentras, L'Isle, the Volscians <note>original: "Volce." Likely referring to the territory of the Volcae, an ancient people of Languedoc, or specifically the area around Vaucluse.</note>, and Mont Cavaillon,

->LXXVII.<-

All the degrees of ecclesiastical honor,
Will be changed into the Dial Quirinal <term>Dial Quirinal: References to the Roman Flamen Dialis (priest of Jupiter) and the Quirinal hill, suggesting a return to pagan-style titles or a total Romanization of the Church.</term>:
Into Martian Quirinal Flaminic <term>Flaminique: Relating to the Flamines, high priests of ancient Rome dedicated to specific gods like Mars.</term>,
Then a King of France will make it Vulcanic <note>original: "vulcanal," referring to Vulcan, the god of fire; implying the King will destroy these institutions through fire or war.</note>.

->LXXVIII.<-

The two united will not hold for long,
And within thirteen years, to the Barbarian Satrap <term>Satrap: Originally a provincial governor in the ancient Persian Empire; here used to denote a powerful Eastern or Ottoman leader.</term>:
On both sides they will suffer such loss,
That one will bless the Bark and his cope <note>The "Bark" (Barque) is the traditional symbol for the Church (the Barque of Saint Peter), and the "cope" (cappe) is a ceremonial papal vestment.</note>.

->LXXIX.<-

The sacred pomp will come to lower its wings,
By the coming of the great legislator:
He will raise the humble, he will vex the rebels,
No rival <note>original: "æmulateur."</note> will be born upon the earth.

Logmion <note>Often interpreted as a printer's error for "Ogmion," referring to Ogmios, the Gallic Hercules, a common symbol for a powerful French monarch in 16th-century literature.</note> will approach great Byzantium <term>Biſance: Constantinople, the heart of the Ottoman Empire.</term>,
The Barbarian league will be driven out:
Of the two laws, the pagan one will fail <note>original: "l'eſtinique lachera." "Estinique" is likely a corruption of "ethnique," used in the Renaissance to mean pagan or non-Christian.</note>,
