# CENTURY VIII.

...the sea, mother [of the deed], will be the cause,
...[con]spirator, and reigns in great remorse.

### LXXIIII.

Into the <term>new land</term> <note>original: "terre neufue"; this could refer to newly discovered territories like the Americas, or simply a recently conquered region</note> the King has entered deep,
Where subjects will come to welcome him:
He will encounter such perfidy <note>deceit or betrayal</note>,
[That it will be] to the citizens a place of celebration and refuge.

### LXXV.

Father and son will be murdered together,
The <term>governor</term> <note>original: "prefecteur"; a high-ranking administrative official</note> inside his pavilion:
The mother at Tours will have her belly swollen with a son,
Amidst the greenery of butterfly-like leaves.

### LXXVI.

More a <term>butcher</term> <note>original: "Macelin"; derived from the Latin *macellum* (meat market or shambles). Many historical commentators link this specific quatrain to the rise of Oliver Cromwell in England</note> than a King in England,
Born obscurely, he will seize the empire by force:
[Cowardly], without faith or law, he will bleed the land,
The time approaches so near that I sigh.

### LXXVII.

The <term>Antichrist</term> <note>original: "Antrechiſt"; a figure representing the ultimate enemy of Christ in apocalyptic tradition</note> will soon see three annihilated,
Twenty-seven years his bloody war will last:
The heretics dead, the captives exiled,
Blood, human bodies, water reddened, hail upon the earth.

### LXXVIII.

<term>Bragamas</term> <note>A cryptic name or anagram that has puzzled scholars; it may refer to a specific person or a play on "Braganza"</note> with the twisted tongue,
Will come to the sanctuary of the gods:
To the heretics he will open the door,
Reviving the <term>Church Militant</term> <note>original: "egliſe militaire"; the portion of the Christian Church on Earth that is actively engaged in spiritual warfare against sin and evil</note>.

### LXXIX.

He who by the sword will lose his father, born of a <term>nun</term> <note>original: "Nonnaire"; a woman belonging to a religious order</note>,

# CENTURY VIII.

...Of the Gorgon’s blood being shed upon [it],
In a foreign land everything will be so silent,
That he will burn himself and his <term>child</term> <note>original: "entant," likely a variant of *enfant* (child) or *entente* (intent/understanding)</note>.

-> LXXX. <-

The blood of the innocent, of widow and virgin,
So many evils committed by means of the great <term>Red One</term> <note>original: "Roge"; in the context of the 16th century, this often referred to the "red hat" of a Cardinal or a figure of high ecclesiastical power</note>,
Holy images dipped in burning candles,
Through fear and dread, none will be seen to move.

-> LXXXI. <-

The new empire in desolation,
Will be changed by the <term>northern pole</term> <note>original: "pole aquilonaire"; derived from *Aquilo*, the Roman personification of the North Wind. This usually refers to Northern European powers or Russia</note>:
From Sicily will come the disturbance,
To trouble the enterprise of <term>Philip's</term> <note>Likely King Philip II of Spain, whose Mediterranean interests were frequently challenged</note> tributary.

-> LXXXII. <-

Thin, tall, and dry, playing the good servant,
In the end will have nothing but his dismissal,
Sharp poison, and letters at his collar,
He will be seized, having escaped into danger.

-> LXXXIII. <-

The greatest sail out of the port of <term>Zara</term> <note>Modern-day Zadar, on the coast of Croatia</note>,
Near <term>Byzantium</term> <note>original: "Biſance"; Istanbul</note> will be its enterprise:
Loss to the enemy, and the friend will not be [found],
The third party to the two will cause great pillage and capture.

-> LXXXIIII. <-

<term>Paterno</term> <note>original: "Paterne"; a town in Sicily located at the foot of Mount Etna</note> will hear the cry from Sicily,
All the preparations in the Gulf of Trieste:
Which will be heard as far as <term>Trinacria</term> <note>An ancient Greek name for Sicily, meaning "three-pointed"</note>,
From so many sails, flee, flee the horrible plague.

-> LXXXV. <-

# CENTURY VIII.

<warning>The left edge of the text is partially obscured or cut off due to the tight binding of the manuscript, affecting the first few letters of the top lines.</warning>

[Betw]een Bayonne and at Saint-Jean-de-Luz,
[Will be] placed the promontory of Mars:
[From the] <term>North</term> <note>original: "Aquilon"; the Latin-derived name for the North Wind or northern regions</note> <term>Hanix</term> <note>meaning uncertain; possibly a proper name or an anagram</note> will take away the light from <term>Nanar</term> <note>meaning uncertain; possibly a reference to a specific person or location</note>,
[Then] suffocated in bed without help.

### LXXXVI.

Through <term>Hernani</term> <note>original: "arnani"; a town in the Basque Country of Spain</note>, Toulouse, a <term>free city</term> <note>original: "Ville Franque"; a city historically granted certain exemptions or privileges</note>,
An infinite band by <term>Mount Adrian</term> <note>original: "le mont Adrian"; the San Adrian pass in the Pyrenees mountains</note>,
Passes the river, a skirmish by the plank bridge,
Entering Bayonne, all shouting "<term>Bigoro</term>!" <note>original: "Bichoro"; likely referring to the people of Bigorre, a region in the Pyrenees</note>.

### LXXXVII.

Conspired death will come to full effect,
The charge given and a journey of death:
Elected, created, received, defeated by his own.
Blood of innocence before him through remorse.

### LXXXVIII.

Into <term>Sardinia</term> a noble King will come,
Who will hold the Kingdom for only three years,
He will join many colors with himself,
He himself afterwards, [his] care and sleep saddened by <term>mockery</term> <note>original: "scome"; from the Latin *scomma*, meaning a taunt or stinging joke</note>.

### LXXXIX.

In order not to fall into the hands of his uncle,
Who slaughtered his children to reign:
Praying to the people, setting foot on <term>Peloncle</term> <note>meaning uncertain; likely a specific stone or geographical location</note>,
Dead and dragged between armored horses.

### XC.

When the <term>crusaders</term> <note>original: "croisez"</note> find one of troubled mind,
In the place of the sacred rite, he will see a horned ox:
By the virgin pig his place will then be filled,
By the King, order will no longer be maintained.

<header>CENTURY VIII.</header>

->XCVII.<-

At the borders of the <term>Var</term> <note>a river and region in southeastern France</note> to change the <term>Pompotans</term> <note>original: "Pompotans"; a neologism likely derived from the Latin "potentans," meaning those who are all-powerful or the "potentates"</note>,
Near the shore the three beautiful children are born:

->XCVIII.<-

The blood of churchmen shall be shed,
In as great an abundance as water:
And for a long time it shall not be stayed,
Woe, woe to the cleric, ruin and grief.

->XCIX.<-

By the power of the three temporal Kings,
The <term>Holy See</term> <note>the jurisdiction and seat of the Pope</note> shall be moved to another place:
Where the substance of the bodily spirit,
Shall be restored and received as the true seat.

->C.<-

Because of the abundance of the <term>army</term> <note>original: "l'arme"; potentially a typo for "larme" (tear), which would change the meaning to "the abundance of tears shed"</note> spread out,
From the high to the low and from the low to the highest:
Too great a faith, life lost through a game,
To die of thirst through an abundant failure.

<header>CENTURY VIII.</header>

But great leisure shall not be given to them.

->II.<-
Several shall come and speak of peace,
Between Monarchs and very powerful lords:
But it shall not be agreed upon so closely,
Unless they render themselves more obedient than others.

->III.<-
Alas, what fury! Alas, what pity,
There shall be among many people:
One never saw such a friendship,
As the wolves shall have in their diligent running. <note>This likely uses "wolves" as a metaphor for predatory or aggressive leaders or soldiers.</note>

->IIII.<-
Many people will want to <term>parley</term> <note>original: "parlementer"; to hold a discussion with an enemy or opposing side to discuss terms</note>,
With the great lords who will make war upon them:
No one will want to listen to them in the least,
Alas! if God does not send peace to the earth.

->V.<-
Many reinforcements shall come from all sides,
From distant people who will want to resist:
They shall all of a sudden be very hurried,
But they will not be able to assist at this hour.

->VI.<-
Alas, what desire foreign Princes have,
Take good care that they do not come into your country:
There would be terrible dangers
In many lands, even in <term>Vienna</term> <note>original: "la Vienne"; this may refer to the city of Vienna in Austria, or the Vienne river and department in western France</note>.

# PROPHECIES OF
## *M. Nostradamus.*
### CENTURY IX.

->I.<-

In the house of the translator of Boure, <note>Commentators often debate if "Boure" refers to a specific individual or the town of Bourg-en-Bresse.</note>
The letters will be found on the table:
One-eyed, red-white, hoary-headed <note>original: "chenu"; meaning grey or white-haired with age.</note> shall hold the court:
Who will change for the new <term>Constable</term> <note>original: "Conestable"; the highest military rank in the French monarchy, serving as the Commander-in-Chief of the army.</note>.

->II.<-

From the top of the Aventine Hill <note>One of the seven hills of ancient Rome, often associated with the common people or religious portents.</note> a voice heard,
Be gone, be gone from both sides:
By the blood of the red ones shall the anger be sated,
From Rimini, Prato, and the Colonna <note>original: "Arimin Prato, Columna"; referring to the Italian cities of Rimini and Prato, and the Colonna family, a powerful noble house in Rome.</note> ousted.

->III.<-

From the great cow <note>original: "magna vaqua"; a Latin phrase. This may be a symbolic reference to a heraldic animal, a statue, or a corruption of a place name.</note> at Ravenna great troubles,
Led by fifteen [men] in irons at Fornase:
In Rome shall be born two monsters with double heads,
Blood, fire, deluge, the greatest ones in the expanse.

->IIII.<-

In the ensuing year discovered by deluge,
Two chiefs elected, the first will not hold:
To flee into the shadow shall be the refuge for one of them,
The house <note>original: "case"; likely from the Italian "casa," meaning house or estate.</note> that first maintains him shall be sacked.

<summary>This page introduces Century IX, featuring quatrains that blend political upheaval in Italy with cryptic omens, including the birth of monsters in Rome and the downfall of high-ranking officials like the Constable.</summary>
<keywords>Aventine Hill, Ravenna, Colonna family, Constable, Rome, omens, Rimini, Prato</keywords>

--- Page 179 ---
<meta>This page continues Century IX of the Prophecies. The preceding quatrains (I-IIII) touched on themes of discovery, Roman portents, and the election of unstable leaders. Quatrains V through X continue this trend, shifting focus toward specific regional conflicts in France and Italy, as well as cryptic warnings regarding the opening of ancient tombs and the rise of low-born monarchs.</meta>

### V.

The third toe of the foot <note>original: "Tiers doibt du pied"; some interpreters suggest this refers to a specific physical deformity or a symbolic "third limb" of power.</note> shall seem to the first
To be a new Monarch, rising from low to high:
He who shall occupy Pisa and Lucca as a Tyrant,
To correct the fault of his predecessor.

### VI.

Throughout Guyenne <note>A historical province in southwest France.</note> an infinity of English,
Shall occupy under the name of *Anglaquitaine*: <note>A portmanteau of "Anglais" (English) and "Aquitaine."</note>
Of Languedoc, the palm of Bordeaux, <note>original: "l. palme Bourdelois"; "L" may represent the Roman numeral for 50, or "la palme" may refer to a victory or a specific measurement.</note>
Which they shall name afterwards *Barboxitaine*. <note>Another portmanteau, likely combining "Barbarian" and "Occitania" (the region of southern France).</note>

### VII.

He who shall open the monument when it is found,
And does not come to close it promptly:
Evil shall come to him, and it cannot be proven,
If it would be better to be a Breton or a Norman King. <note>This quatrain reflects a common Renaissance-era superstition that disturbing ancient tombs brought curses upon the living.</note>

### VIII.

A younger son <term>made King</term> <note>original: "Puisnay Roy fait"; a "puisné" is a younger brother, usually not destined for the throne.</note> puts his father to death,
After a conflict of a most dishonorable death:
A discovered writing shall give remorse to suspicion,
When the hunted wolf rests upon the cot.

### IX.

When the burning lamp of inextinguishable fire,
Shall be found in the temple of the Vestals: <note>The Vestal Virgins were priestesses of ancient Rome who guarded a sacred, eternal fire.</note>
A foundling child; fire, water passing through a sieve,
Nîmes to perish by water, the market halls of Toulouse to fall.

### X.

Monk and nun, of a dead child exposed, <note>meaning an abandoned infant.</note>
To die by a she-bear and carried off by a glassmaker:

<summary>This page contains quatrains V through X of Century IX, focusing on the rise of a self-made tyrant in Italy, English incursions into southern France, and the dire consequences of disturbing ancient tombs.</summary>
<keywords>Monarch, Pisa, Lucca, Guyenne, Anglaquitaine, Languedoc, Bordeaux, Barboxitaine, Breton, Norman, Vestals, Nîmes, Toulouse</keywords>

# NINTH CENTURY

By Foix and Pamiers the camp shall be set,
Against Toulouse, and Carcassonne shall set out scouts.

->XI.<-

The just man shall be wrongly put to death,
Publicly, and his light extinguished in their midst:
So great a plague shall be born in this place,
That the judges shall be forced to flee. <note>This quatrain follows a common theme in Renaissance literature: that the execution of an innocent man brings divine retribution in the form of disease or natural disaster.</note>

->XII.<-

The vast amount of silver of Diana and Mercury, <note>Diana and Mercury likely refer to the metals silver and quicksilver (mercury) in an alchemical or symbolic sense, or to ancient statues of the Roman deities.</note>
The statues shall be found in the lake:
The potter seeking new clay,
He and his people shall be drenched in gold.

->XIII.<-

The Exiles around the Sologne,
Led by night to march into the Auxois, <note>A traditional territory in Burgundy.</note>
Two from Modena, fierce ones from Bologna,
Put to flight, discovered by the fire of Buzançais. <note>original: "Burançóis"; likely referring to Buzançais in the Indre region of France.</note>

->XIIII.<-

Placed on the plain, the cauldron of the <term>dyers</term>, <note>original: "infecteurs"; those who work with stains, dyes, or chemicals. In this context, it may suggest a primitive chemical weapon or a large-scale industrial accident.</note>
Wine, honey, and oil, and built upon furnaces:
They shall be plunged into it, without being called evildoers,
Seven [blasts of] smoke extinguished at the mouth of the conduits.

->XV.<-

Near Perpignan <note>original: "Parpan"</note> the <term>reds</term> <note>Likely referring to Cardinals of the Church or soldiers wearing red uniforms.</note> are detained,
Those of the center sunken and led far away:
Three cut to pieces, and five poorly supported,
For the Lord and Prelate of Burgundy.

->XVI.<-

From Castelfranco the assembly shall emerge.

<summary>This page describes a series of localized conflicts and strange omens, including the discovery of hidden treasure by a potter and a plague following a wrongful execution. The geographic focus remains on southern France and northern Italy, characteristic of Nostradamus's regional preoccupations.</summary>
<keywords>Nostradamus, prophecy, Toulouse, Perpignan, Diana, Mercury, alchemy, plague, judicial injustice, Burgundy</keywords>

# NINTH CENTURY

The unpleasant ambassador shall cause a schism,
Those of Rivière <note>original: "Ribiere"; likely a family name or a reference to a riverbank community.</note> shall be in the fray:
And at the great gulf they are denied entry.

->XVII.<-

The first of the third <note>likely referring to the "Third Estate" or the third ruler of a series.</note> shall do worse than Nero did,
As valiant in emptying [veins] as shedding human blood:
He will cause the baker <note>original: "forneron"; literally one who tends an oven, sometimes used metaphorically for someone of low birth or a specific trade.</note> to rebuild,
A golden age, death, a new King, and a great scandal.

->XVIII.<-

The Dauphinois lily <note>original: "Le lys Dauffois"; referring to the lily of the Dauphin, the heir to the French throne.</note> shall be carried into Nancy,
Even into Flanders, the Elector of the Empire:
A new [gate] blocked to the great Montmorency,
Delivered to certain punishment outside of proven places.

->XIX.<-

In the middle of the forest of Mayenne,
When the Sun is in Leo <note>original: "Sol au Lyon"; an astrological timing indicating the month of July/August.</note> the lightning shall fall:
The great bastard born of the great man of Maine,
On this day Fougères shall enter, the point [of the sword] in blood.

->XX.<-

By night he shall come through the forest of Rennes,
By a winding way, a pair, <term>Herne</term> the white stone: <note>The "white stone" has been interpreted by some as a reference to the "Petra Blanca" or a landmark near the site of a royal escape.</note>
The black monk in gray within Varennes,
A chosen captain causes tempest, fire, and blood to slice. <note>This quatrain is famously associated by historians with the Flight to Varennes (1791), where King Louis XVI (the "black monk") was captured while wearing a gray disguise.</note>

->XXI.<-

At the high temple of Blois, the consecration of Salonne,
At night, the bridge of the Loire, Prelate, and a destructive King: <note>original: "pernicant"; from the Latin *pernicare*, to kill or destroy.</note>
A crushing victory at the marshes of the Lone,
Whence the prelacy of those in white is failing. <note>original: "abormeant"; likely meaning failing, aborting, or vanishing.</note>

->XXII.<-

<summary>This page features several cryptic quatrains focused on French dynastic struggles and regional conflicts, including the famous "Varennes" prophecy often linked to the French Revolution. The verses emphasize themes of betrayal, royal flight, and religious-political shifts in cities like Nancy, Blois, and Varennes.</summary>
<keywords>Nero, Dauphin, Nancy, Montmorency, Mayenne, Fougères, Varennes, Louis XVI, Blois, Loire, French Revolution, prophecy</keywords>

# NINTH CENTURY

The King and his court in the place of the <term>Albe</term> tongue <note>original: "langue halbe"; scholars debate if this refers to the Duke of Alva, the "Langue d'oc" region, or the ancient name for Scotland (Alba).</note>,
Inside the temple across from the palace:
In the garden, the Duke of Montor and of Alva,
Alva and Montor, dagger, tongue, and palace.

->XXIII.<-

The younger son playing in the cool air beneath the barrel <note>original: "la tonne"; refers to a large wine vat or barrel.</note>,
The top of the roof from the middle [falls] upon his head:
The father King in the temple of Saint Solonne <note>The Cathedral of Saint-Solenne is located in Blois, France.</note>,
Sacrificing, he will consecrate with the smoke of the festival.

->XXIIII.<-

At the palace by the rocky windows,
The two little royals shall be snatched away:
To pass through Aurelle, <term>Lutetia</term> <note>original: "Luthece"; the ancient Roman name for Paris</note>, and the Saint-Denis cloisters,
Fadonnain, <term>mollods</term> <note>possibly "mastiffs" or "Molossians," often interpreted as a cryptic reference to a specific group of people</note> swallowing green pits.

->XXV.<-

Passing the bridges, to come near the rosebushes,
Arrived late, sooner than he will think:
The new Spaniards shall come to Béziers,
Which shall break this hunt and enterprise.

->XXVI.<-

Nice [having] departed under the name of harsh letters,
The great <term>cappe</term> <note>original: "grande cappe"; likely referring to the "Cappa" or cape worn by high church officials, or the Capetian dynasty</note> will make a gift not his own:
Near Vultry by the walls of green capers,
After Plombino <note>original: "plombin"; a town in Tuscany</note>, the wind in good earnest.

->XXVII.<-

The guard of wood, the wind shut, the bridge will be broken,
Received on high, he will strike the Dauphin <note>the title given to the heir apparent to the French throne.</note>,
The old <term>teccon</term> <note>a highly debated term; possibly meaning "architect," "silent one," or a reference to a specific historical figure</note> shall pass through the joined woods,
Passing further beyond the Duke's rightful border.

<keywords>Nostradamus, Duke of Alva, Lutetia, Paris, Dauphin, Béziers, Saint-Solenne, prophecies</keywords>

# NINTH CENTURY

## XXVIII.

The sail of <term>Symmachus</term> <note>original: "Voille Symacle"; Symmachus was an early Pope (498–514 AD). The "sail" often refers to the bark of the Church or maritime influence of the Papacy.</note> [at the] port of Marseille,
In Venice, the port to march toward the <term>Pannonians</term> <note>original: "Pannons"; refers to the inhabitants of Pannonia, an ancient Roman province located in the modern-day Balkans and Hungary.</note>:
To depart from the gulf and the Illyrian <term>Sinus</term> <note>original: "Synus Illirique"; refers to the Adriatic Sea coast of modern Croatia/Albania.</note>,
Devastation to Sicily, <term>Ligurian</term> <note>The region around Genoa, Italy.</note> cannon shots.

## XXIX.

When he who gives way to no one <note>original: "celuy qu’à nul ne donne lieu"; This is widely considered a reference to the motto of Holy Roman Emperor Charles V, *Nulli Cedo* ("I yield to none").</note>,
Will wish to abandon a place [whether] taken or not taken:
Fire [in the] ship through the marshes <note>original: "saignes"; can refer to bloodletting, trenches, or marshy drains.</note>, bitumen at Charlieu,
Saint-Quentin and Basel shall be retaken.

## XXX.

At the port of <term>Pola</term> <note>original: "Pvola"; a port city on the Istrian peninsula, now Pula in Croatia.</note> and of Saint Nicholas,
Danger to the Normans in the <term>Phanatic</term> gulf <note>original: "goulfre Phanatique"; likely referring to the Gulf of Fano on the Adriatic coast.</note>:
The captain of Byzantium <note>original: "Bisance"; the city of Constantinople or modern-day Istanbul.</note> [in the] streets crying "alas,"
Help from Cádiz <note>original: "Gaddes"; the ancient Phoenician and Spanish port.</note> and from the great <term>Philip</term> <note>original: "grand Philipique"; likely a reference to King Philip II of Spain.</note>.

## XXXI.

The earthquake at Mortara,
Tin <note>original: "Cassich"; likely derived from the Greek *kassiteros* for tin, or perhaps a cryptic reference to a person or the town of Cassis.</note> [at] Saint George half-submerged:
Peace [being] drowsy, the war shall awaken,
In the temple at Easter, abysses shall open.

## XXXII.

A deep column of fine porphyry <note>A prestigious purple-red igneous rock used in Imperial Roman monuments.</note> found,
Beneath the slab, Capitoline writings:
Bones, twisted hair, Roman strength proven,
The fleet <note>original: "Classe"; from the Latin *classis*.</note> to be stirred at the port of <term>Mytilene</term> <note>original: "Methelin"; the capital of the Greek island of Lesbos.</note>.

## XXXIII.

Hercules, King of Rome and of Denmark,
Three times surnamed of Gaul, <term>Guion</term> <note>original: "Guion"; interpreted by some as a reference to Geryon, the mythological giant, or a cryptic name for the House of Guise.</note>:
Italy shall tremble and the one of Saint Mark <note>A reference to Venice, whose symbol is the Winged Lion of Saint Mark.</note>,
The first above all, a renowned monarch.

<summary>This page describes various naval movements and geopolitical conflicts involving Venice, Marseille, and the Ottoman Empire (Byzantium), alongside cryptic omens like earthquakes and the discovery of ancient Roman relics. It highlights the influence of major historical figures, notably referring to Emperor Charles V and King Philip II of Spain.</summary>
<keywords>Venice, Marseille, Adriatic Sea, Charles V, Philip II, earthquakes, Roman antiquities, maritime warfare, Pannonians, Porphyry</keywords>

# CENTURY IX.

### XLV.

**The** great **MENDOSUS** <note>Often considered an anagram for "Vendosme" (the Bourbon-Vendôme line), referring to the future Henry IV of France.</note> will obtain his empire:
[The decree] of the court will be countermanded from Ubique <note>original: "vbing"; likely a corruption of the Latin *ubique*, meaning "everywhere," or a specific court location.</note>,
**Piedmont**, **Picardy**. **Paris** [will find] the **Tuscan** <note>original: "Tyrhen"; a reference to the Tyrrhenian/Tuscan origin of Catherine de' Medici.</note> the worst.

### XLVI.

Leave, flee from **Toulouse**, [you] in red <note>Often interpreted as the red-robed jurists of the Parliament of Toulouse or Cardinals.</note>,
To make expiation for the sacrifice:
The chief of evil under the shadow of the gourds,
Dead by strangling, a fleshly omen <note>original: "carne omination"; a Latin-style construction suggesting a prophecy of the flesh.</note>.

### XLVI.
<note>The original text repeats the number 46, though this is the 47th quatrain.</note>

The fools marked by an unworthy deliverance,
And they will have a counter-opinion regarding the fine <note>original: "mulre"; likely a misspelling of the French *mulcte* (a fine or penalty).</note>;
The monarch changed, placed in perilous thought,
Locked in a cage, they will see each other face to face.

### XLVIII.

The great city of the maritime **Ocean**,
Surrounded by marshes of crystal:
During the winter solstice and the spring,
It will be tested by a terrifying wind.

### XLIX.

**Ghent** & **Brussels** will march against **Antwerp**,
The **Senate of London** will put their **King** to death:
Salt and wine will be turned upside down for him,
For them to have the kingdom in disarray.

### L.

**MENDOSUS** will soon come to his high reign,
Putting back a little the **Norlaris** <note>Widely interpreted as an anagram for "Lorrains," the powerful House of Guise, rivals to the Bourbons.</note>:
The red one turns pale, the male in the interregnum,
The young one [filled with] fear and **Barbarian** terror.

### L I.

<sig>D</sig>

<summary>This page contains several of Nostradamus's most famous political predictions, including a clear reference to the execution of King Charles I by the English Parliament and the rise of the Bourbon dynasty (Mendosus) over the House of Guise (Norlaris).</summary>

<meta>This page continues the quatrains of Century IX, picking up from the final lines of Quatrain LXXIV. # CENTURY IX.

Done and done, much fine [water?] <note>original: "beu ſarant"; a difficult phrase possibly referring to "beu" (beautiful/fine) and "serant" (flowing or sacrificial water).</note> not to slaughter <note>original: "macter"; from the Latin "mactare," to sacrifice or slaughter.</note>:
Return once more to the honors of Artemis <note>The Greek goddess of the hunt and the moon, known as Diana to the Romans.</note>,
And to Vulcan <note>The Roman god of fire and the forge.</note> to bury the dead bodies.

->LXXV.<-

From Ambracia <note>An ancient city in Epirus, Greece.</note> and the land of Thrace,
People [suffering] harm by sea and [receiving] Gallic <note>French</note> help:
The trace remains perpetual in Provence,
With vestiges of their customs and laws.

->LXXVI.<-

With the black Rapacious and bloodthirsty one,
Issued from the pallet <note>original: "peaultre"; a truckle bed or pallet, often implying a base or scandalous birth.</note> of the inhuman Nero:
Between two rivers, the military left hand,
Will be murdered by a young bald-pate <note>original: "Ioyne chaulueron"; "Ioyne" likely refers to the House of Joyeuse or simply "young," while "chaulueron" suggests a bald head or a specific red cap.</note>.

->LXXVII.<-

The realm taken, the King will invite,
The lady taken [by those] sworn to death <note>original: "mortiurez"; those who have taken a blood oath or are condemned.</note> at the fort:
Life will be denied to the Queen’s son,
And the mistress <note>original: "pellix"; from the Latin "pellex," meaning a concubine or rival wife.</note> at the fort of the consort <note>original: "confort"; can mean strength, comfort, or a spouse/consort.</note>.

->LXXVIII.<-

The Greek lady of Lais-like beauty <note>original: "laydique"; an allusion to Lais of Corinth, a famous and beautiful courtesan of antiquity.</note>,
Made happy by innumerable lawsuits:
Carried out into the Spanish realm,
Taken captive to die a miserable death.

->LXXIX.<-

The chief of the fleet <note>original: "chef de claſſe"; from the Latin "classis," meaning a fleet of ships.</note>, by fraud and stratagem,
Will make the timid ones come out of their galleys:
Having come out, they are murdered; the chief, a renegade of the chrism <note>original: "renieux de creſme"; likely referring to an apostate who has renounced his Christian anointing or "cream."</note>,
Then by ambush they will pay him his wages.

# CENTURY IX.

The Duke will wish to exterminate his own,
He will send the strongest to strange places:
Through tyranny Bize and Luc <note>Likely referring to Bize-Minervois and Luc-sur-Orbieu, villages in southern France.</note> are ruined,
Then the Barbarians will harvest without wine.

-> L X X X I <-

The cunning King will understand his ambushes,
Attacking enemies from three quarters:
A strange number of "coqueluche" <note>original: "coqueluches"; this term historically referred to a specific type of hood or a popular fashion, but also to an epidemic of whooping cough. In this context, it suggests a mass of people affected by a trend or a sickness.</note> tears,
The printer’s mark <note>original: "Lemprin du traducteur"; likely a contraction of "l'emprin" (the imprint/impression) or "l'empereur" (the emperor). It suggests a failure in the recorded word or the leadership.</note> will come to fail.

-> L X X X I I . <-

Through the flood and strong pestilence,
The great city long besieged:
The sentinel and guard of "mortmain" <note>original: "main morte"; a legal term referring to "dead hand" or inalienable property, often held by the church or a corporation that does not die.</note>,
Suddenly taken, but outraged by no one.

-> L X X X I I I . <-

When the Sun is twenty degrees into Taurus <note>Late April or May.</note> the earth will tremble so strongly,
The great theater, being full, will be ruined:
The air, sky, and earth darkened and troubled,
Then the infidel will call upon <note>original: "voguera"; likely a variation of "invoquera" (to invoke/call upon) or "voguera" (to drift/travel).</note> God and the saints.

-> L X X X I I I I . <-

The exposed King will complete the hecatomb <note>original: "hecatombe"; a great public sacrifice or slaughter, historically involving the sacrifice of one hundred oxen.</note>,
After having discovered his origin:
A torrent will open the tomb of marble and lead,
Of a great Roman with Medusine <note>original: "Meduſine"; referring to the Gorgon Medusa, often used as an emblem on Roman shields and armor.</note> insignia.

-> L X X X V . <-

Passing through Guienne, Languedoc, and the Rhône,
From Agen holding Marmande and La Réole:
To open through faith, the Phocaean <note>original: "Phocẽ"; referring to the people of Phocaea who founded Marseille.</note> wall will hold its throne,
Conflict near Saint-Paul-de-Mausole <note>original: "ſainct pol de Manſeole"; a monastery and asylum in Provence.</note>.

# CENTURY IX.

### LXXXVI.

From Bourg-la-Reine <note>A town just south of Paris.</note> they will come straight to Chartres,
And they will pause near the bridge of Antony:
Seven for peace, as cunning as martens <note>original: "Martres"; the marten is a weasel-like animal traditionally associated with cleverness or stealth.</note>,
Will make an entry into Paris with a closed army. <insert>1</insert>

### LXXXVII.

Through the cleared forest of Touphon <note>Likely referring to the forest of Torfou.</note>,
The temple will be placed by the hermitage:
The Duke of Etampes, by his invented ruse,
The prelate of Montlhéry <note>original: "mont Lehori"; Montlhéry is a town south of Paris known for its ancient tower.</note> will provide an example. <insert>3</insert>

### LXXXVIII.

Calais, Arras, help for Thérouanne,
Peace and pretense will feign the scouting:
The soldiery of the Allobroges <note>original: "Alobrox"; refers to the people of the Savoy region.</note> will descend by Roanne,
Diverted people who will desert the route. <insert>4</insert>

### LXXXIX.

For seven years the fortune of **PHILIP** will be prosperous,
He will strike down the effort of the **BARBARIANS** <note>Historically refers to the Ottoman or North African forces.</note>:
Then his noon <note>A metaphor for the middle or peak of his life/reign.</note> will be perplexed, a backward affair,
A young Ogmion <note>An allegorical name used by Nostradamus to signify a powerful leader, derived from the Celtic god of eloquence, Ogmios.</note> will ruin his strength. <insert>5</insert>

### XC.

A Captain from great Germany,
Will come to surrender through feigned help:
To the King of Kings help from Pannonia <note>original: "Pannonie"; an ancient Roman province covering parts of modern-day Hungary, Austria, and the Balkans.</note>,
Such that the revolt will cause a great flow of blood. <insert>6</insert>

### XCI.

The horrible plague at Perinthus and Nicopolis <note>Ancient cities in the Thrace and Balkan regions.</note>,
Will take hold of the Chersonese <note>Likely the Gallipoli peninsula.</note> and Macedonia:
Thessaly will devastate Amphipolis <note>original: "l'amphipolle"; an ancient Greek city.</note>, <insert>8</insert>

# CENTURY IV.

Despairing, searching for the Elysian Fields <note>original: "champs Helisées"; in Greek mythology, the final resting place of the souls of the heroic and virtuous.</note>,
Those who are first to enter the breaches will have victory.

->XCVIII.<-

Those afflicted by the fault of a single tainted person,
Opposing the rival party:
To the people of Lyon <note>original: "Lygonnois"; a reference to the inhabitants of Lyon, France, derived from its Latin name, Lugdunum.</note> he will send word that, constrained,
They will be forced to surrender the great leader of Malta <note>original: "Molite"; likely referring to the island of Malta (Latin: Melita).</note>.

->XCIX.<-

The North Wind <term>Aquilon: The classical personification of the cold North wind.</term> will cause the siege to be raised,
Over the walls they will throw ashes, lime, and dust:
Afterwards, by rain which will be a snare for them,
The final help [will be] against their border.

->C.<-

The naval battle <note>original: "Naualle pugne"; from the Latin 'navalis pugna'.</note> will be overcome by night,
The fire in the ships will bring ruin to the West:
A new rubric <term>Rubric: Traditionally a heading or set of instructions written in red ink; here, it suggests a new law, decree, or political order.</term>, the great ship colored,
Anger to the vanquished, and victory in the mist.

# THE PROPHECIES OF
## M. Nostradamus.
### CENTURY X.

->I.<-

To the enemy, to the enemy, the promised faith <note>original: "foy"; in this context, it refers to a pledge, a truce, or a sworn oath.</note>,
Will not be kept, the captives [being] detained:
One taken near death, and the rest in their shirts <note>original: "en chemise"; a traditional idiom for having lost everything, being stripped of armor and wealth.</note>,
Giving what remains in order to be rescued.

->II.<-

The galley’s sail will hide the ship’s sail,
The great fleet <term>Classe: Derived from the Latin 'classis', meaning a naval fleet.</term> will cause the lesser one to depart:
Ten nearby ships will turn to drive it back,
The great [ship] defeated, joined together in a common faith.

->III.<-

Afterwards, five will not send out the flock,
A fugitive will let loose for Penelon <note>A cryptic name; scholars often debate if this refers to a specific individual or a corruption of a place name like Piombino (Latin: Populonium).</note>:
Falsely whispering that help arrives at that time,
The leader will then abandon the siege.

->IV.<-

Toward midnight, the leader of the army
Will save himself, suddenly vanished:
Seven years later, his reputation unblemished,
Upon his return, he will never say yes.

<keywords>Century X, broken oaths, naval warfare, fleet, Penelon, military leadership, siege</keywords>

# CENTURY X

->V.<-

Albi and Castres will form a new league,
Nine Arians <note>Followers of a 4th-century heresy, though Nostradamus often used the term to describe contemporary Protestants or heretics.</note>, Lisbon and the Portuguese:
Carcassonne and Toulouse will exhaust their intrigue <note>original: "brigue"; refers to factional plotting or political maneuvering.</note>,
When a new leader [appears] as a portent from Lauragais <note>A historical region in southern France; "monstre" in the original can mean a physical monster or a divine omen.</note>.

->VI.<-

The Gardon and Nîmes <note>original: "Sardon Nemans"; likely referring to the Gardon river and the city of Nîmes.</note> will overflow so high,
That they will believe Deucalion <note>The survivor of the Great Flood in Greek mythology, the equivalent of the biblical Noah.</note> has been reborn:
The majority will flee into the colossus <note>A reference to a massive building or monument.</note>,
Vesta’s <note>The Roman goddess of the hearth and home; her temple fire was never supposed to go out.</note> tomb, the extinguished fire will appear.

->VII.<-

The great conflict being prepared at Nancy,
The Amathien <note>original: "L'Æmathien"; a classical epithet for a Macedonian or a conqueror in the vein of Alexander the Great.</note> will say, "I subject all":
The British Isle through wine and salt in folly,
Hem.mi.deux Phi <note>A notoriously cryptic cipher; scholars often debate if it refers to specific monarchs like Henry (Hem) and Philip (Phi), or a specific date.</note> will not hold Metz for long.

->VIII.<-

Index and thumb will pierce the brow,
The Count of Senegalia <note>Modern-day Senigallia on the Adriatic coast of Italy.</note> to his own son:
The Myrmée <note>original: "La Myrnamée"; likely a reference to the Myrmidons, the legendary warriors of Achilles.</note> through several in the first front,
Three wounded to death within seven days.

->IX.<-

From Castillon and Figueres <note>Towns in France and Spain, respectively.</note> on a gloomy day,
From an infamous woman a sovereign prince will be born:
The surname of breeches <note>original: "chausses"; possibly a nickname for a ruler, such as the "House of Valois" or a specific garment-related moniker.</note>... posthumous,
Never was a King so bad in his province.

->X.<-

Stained by murder, enormous adulteries,
Great enemy of the entire human race:
Who will be worse than grandfathers, uncles, or fathers,

# CENTURY X.

By those who do not flatter, you shall be made envious,
King, robbed, [having had] too much faith in the kitchen <note>Nostradamus often uses "the kitchen" as a metaphor for courtly excess or the site of literal poisonings and plots.</note>.

->XVII.<-

The Queen Ergaste <note>A name possibly derived from the Greek "ergates," meaning a worker or artisan, used here perhaps as a cryptic title.</note> seeing her daughter pale,
From a regret enclosed within her breast:
Lamentable cries will then come from Angoulême <note>A city in France; the title "Duke of Angoulême" was frequently held by members of the French royal family.</note>,
And the marriage to the kinsman <term>germain</term> <note>original: "germain"; referring to a close relative, such as a first cousin or a brother.</note> shall be foreclosed.

->XVIII.<-

The high put low, and the low put high:
The son of Hamon <note>Possibly a reference to the biblical Haman, or a classicized version of the name Aymon.</note> shall be elected in Rome,
And the two great ones shall be found wanting.

->XIX.<-

The day that she shall be greeted as Queen,
The day after the greeting, the prayer:
The account being settled and valued <unclear>valbuée</unclear> <note>The word "valbuée" is obscure; it may be a misspelling of "valuable" or a variant of "balbutiée" (stammered/mumbled).</note>,
She who was previously humble was never so proud.

->XX.<-

All the friends who have taken a side,
Put to death and pillaged for being harsh in their letters <note>This could refer to scholars, reformers, or those whose private correspondence was intercepted.</note>:
Goods made public by a fixed and great nothingness,
Never were the Roman people so outraged.

->XXI.<-

By <note>original: "Bar"; likely a typographical error for "Par" (By).</note> the spite of the King supporting the lesser one,
He shall be murdered while presenting the rings to him:
The father wishing to instill nobility in the son,
Does as the Magi <term>Magues</term> <note>The priestly class of ancient Persia, known for their wisdom and king-making influence.</note> once did in Persia.

->XXII.<-

For not wanting to consent to the devoured <note>The quatrain cuts off here; "deuorée" could refer to a person or a situation that consumes or destroys.</note>,

# CENTURY X.

Second and third who make the primary music,
Shall be by the King sublimated in honor:
By the fat and the thin, almost half-emaciated,
The false report of Venus <note>This may refer to the planet, the goddess of love, or metaphorically to a physical ailment associated with the goddess.</note> will make her depressed.

->XXIX.<-

From Saint-Paul-de-Mausole <note>original: "Pol MAN S O L"; likely referring to the monastery of Saint-Paul-de-Mausole near Nostradamus's birthplace of Saint-Rémy-de-Provence.</note> in the goat-like cavern,
Hidden and taken, pulled out by the beard:
Led captive like a mastiff beast,
By the people of Bigorre <term>Begourdans</term> <note>original: "Begourdans"; residents of the Bigorre region in the Pyrenees.</note> led near Tarbes <note>A city in southwestern France.</note>.

->XXX.<-

Nephew and blood of the saintly newcomer,
Through the surname he supports the bows and the shelter <note>original: "arcs & couuert"; possibly a heraldic reference to a coat of arms or a specific family name.</note>:
They shall be hunted, put to death, chased naked,
Into red and black they shall convert their green.

->XXXI.<-

The Holy Empire <note>The Holy Roman Empire.</note> shall come into Germany,
The Ishmaelites <note>A term often used in this period to refer to Arab peoples or the Ottoman Turks.</note> shall find places open:
The Asses shall also want Carmania <note>An ancient province of the Persian Empire, now part of modern-day Iran.</note>,
The supporters all covered by the earth.

->XXXII.<-

The great empire was meant to be for everyone <note>original: "chacun an"; literally "each year," though often interpreted as "belonging to everyone" in this context.</note>,
One shall come to obtain it over the others:
But for a short time shall be his reign and existence,
For two years he shall be able to sustain himself by the ships.

->XXXIII.<-

The cruel faction in the long robes <note>Often interpreted as a reference to the clergy or members of the legal profession.</note>,
Shall come to hide beneath the sharp daggers:
To seize Florence, the Duke, and the double-voiced <term>diphlongue</term> <note>original: "diphlongue"; likely a reference to a "diphthong" or a place name with a double-vowel sound, or perhaps a metaphor for "double-tongued" treachery.</note> place,
Its discovery by mutterers and idlers <note>original: "immeurs & flangnards"; these are obscure 16th-century terms likely referring to gossip-mongers or low-level soldiers.</note>.

<meta>This page continues the Tenth Century of Nostradamus's prophecies. It follows the interrupted quatrain XXXII from the previous page, although that quatrain is not fully repeated here. # CENTURY X.

### XXXIIII.

The Frenchman who shall occupy the empire through war,
Shall be betrayed by his younger brother-in-law:
Dragged by a wild, leaping horse,
For this deed the brother shall be hated for a long time.

### XXXV.

A royal younger son blazing with burning lust,
To enjoy his first cousin:
Dressed in woman’s clothes in the temple of Artemis <note>original: "Arthemide"; the Greek goddess of the hunt and wilderness.</note>,
Going to be murdered by an unknown person from the Marne <note>A river and department in northeastern France.</note>.

### XXXVI.

After the King of the stump <note>original: "ſoucq"; likely "souche," meaning a tree stump or the root of a family tree, often implying a dynastic lineage.</note> speaking of wars,
The Harmotic <note>original: "Harmotique"; a cryptic term often interpreted by scholars as a reference to England (from the Greek "harmos" meaning a joint or "harmonia") or to the "fitting" together of two entities.</note> Isle will hold him in contempt:
For several good years gnawing and pillaging,
By tyranny changing the prize <note>original: "pris"; can mean price, value, or capture.</note> of the island.

### XXXVII.

A great assembly near Lake Bourget <note>original: "lac du Borget"; located in the Savoy region of France.</note>,
They shall rally near Montmélian:
Passing further on, pensive, they shall make a plan,
Chambéry, Maurienne <note>original: "Moriane".</note>, combat at Saint-Julien.

### XXXVIII.

Love <note>Alternatively "The Eagle," as the original "legre" likely refers to l'Aigle.</note> at Aigle <note>A town in Normandy or possibly the Swiss town of the same name.</note> not far off sets the siege,
The garrisons shall be at the holy barbarian:
The Orsini <term>Vrſins</term> <note>A powerful Italian noble family.</note> of the Adriatic shall pledge for the Frenchman,
Surrendered for fear of the army of the Grisons <note>original: "Griſons"; residents of the easternmost canton of Switzerland.</note>.

### XXXIX.

First son of a widow, unhappy marriage,
Without any children, two islands in discord:
Before eighteen, an immature age,

E <note>Signature/Catchword for the next page.</note>

<vocab>Gaulois, Arthemide, Borget, Montmelian, Chambry, Moriane, Sainct-Iulian, Vrſins, Hadrie, Griſons</vocab>

Defeats false pilgrims and ravishers.

### CENTURY X.

[The one] raised up will not recognize his scepter,
He will shame his young children from among the greatest:
Never was there a more foul and cruel being,
For their wives, he will banish them to a dark death.

->L V I I I.<-

In the time of mourning when the feline monarch <note>original: "felin"; possibly a typo for "Selin" (referring to the Ottoman Sultan) or a heraldic reference to the "cats" or lilies of France.</note>

The navy shall shake, the ship shall be in peril,
Tempting the Phocaeans <note>original: "Phossens"; the people of Marseille, which was founded by Phocaean Greeks.</note> with talk in the West.

->L I X.<-

Inside Lyon, twenty-five in a single breath,
Five citizens: Germans, men of Bresse, and Latins:
Beneath a noble, they will lead a long train, <note>original: "traine"; can refer to a procession, a plot, or a physical train of robes.</note>

->L X.<-

I weep for Nice, Monaco, Pisa, Genoa,
Savona, Siena, Capua, Modena, Malta:
Above blood and sword as New Year's gifts, <note>original: "estrennes"; traditional gifts given at the start of the year, here used to describe the "gifts" of war.</note>

->L X I.<-

<unclear>B</unclear>uda, Vienna, Emorre, Sopron, <note>original: "Sacarbance"; the Latin name for Sopron in Hungary.</note>

By pike and fire, enormous violence,
The conspirators discovered by a matron.

Near Sorbin to attack Hungary,
The herald of Buda <note>original: "Brudes"; likely a reference to Buda, the capital of Hungary.</note> will come to warn them:
The Byzantine leader, Salona of Slavonia, <note>Slavonia is a historical region in modern-day Croatia.</note>
The law of the Arabs <note>Referring to the expansion of Islamic law/Ottoman rule.</note> will come to convert them.

->LXIII.<-
Kidron, Ragusa, the city of Saint Jerome, <note>original: "Cydron, Ragufe, la cité au fainct Hieron"; Kidron is a valley in Jerusalem; Ragusa is modern-day Dubrovnik. Saint Jerome was the patron saint of Dalmatia, further linking this to the Adriatic coast.</note>

O vast Rome, your ruin approaches,

That he will plunge them all into the fray.

->LXVII.<-

Saturn in Capricorn, Jupiter and Mercury in Taurus: <note>original: "au bœuf"; literally "at the bull," the astrological sign of Taurus.</note>

Then hail larger than an egg shall fall.

->LXVIII.<-
The naval army shall hold its position before the city,
Then it shall depart without making a long journey:
It shall seize great prey from the citizens on land,

### CENTURY X.

By force of arms, the great gates of bronze
Shall be opened, the King and Duke joining together,
The fort demolished, the ship sunk to the bottom, on a serene day.

->LXXXI.<-

The treasure placed in the temple by Hesperian <note>original: "Hesperiques"; referring to the people of the West, usually signifying Spain or Italy in classical contexts.</note> citizens,
Within it retired in a secret place:
The temple opening, the famished ones break their bonds,
Recaptured, ravished, a horrible prey in their midst.

->LXXXII.<-

Cries, weeping, tears will come with knives,
Seeming to flee, they will give the final assault:
Around the enclosures <note>original: "parques"; can refer to enclosed grounds or fold.</note> planting deep trenches,
The living repulsed and murdered on the spot <term>original: "prinsault"; from "de prime saut," meaning suddenly or at first impulse.</term>.

->LXXXIII.<-

No signal to battle will be given,
They will be forced to leave the enclosure:
Around Ghent <note>original: "Gand"; a city in present-day Belgium.</note> the banner shall be recognized,
Which will put all of its own people to death.

->LXXXIIII.<-

The natural-born one at such a height, high and not low,
The late return will make the grieving content:
The Seclusion <note>original: "Recloing"; potentially referring to a person in retreat or a specific place of confinement.</note> will not be without disputes,
In filling and wasting all of his time.

->LXXXV.<-

The old tribune at the point of the trembling <note>original: "trehemide"; likely a corruption of the Old French for trembling or a specific mechanical device like a hopper.</note>,
Will be hard-pressed, the captive not released:
Willing or unwilling, the timid speaker of ill,
By legitimate means delivered to his friends.

->LXXXVI.<-

### CENTURY X.

->XCII.<-
Before the father the child shall be killed,
The father afterward among ropes of reeds <note>The original French "ionc" (rush/reed) suggests being bound with crude, makeshift bindings.</note>:
The Genevan people shall be hard-pressed,
Leaving the leader in the middle like a trunk <note>Likely referring to a decapitated body or a leader stripped of power and limbs.</note>.

->XCIII.<-
The new ship will receive the travelers,
There and nearby they will transfer the empire:
Beaucaire and Arles will hold the hostages,
Near two columns of porphyry <note>A hard, purple-red stone used in antiquity for imperial monuments; its discovery often symbolized the recovery of ancient Roman glory.</note> found there.

->XCIIII.<-
To scorn Nîmes, Arles, and Vienne,
Not obeying the Western <term>original: "Heſpericque"; derived from Hesperia, a classical name for the West, usually referring to Spain.</term> edict:
To the laborers to condemn the great one,
Six escaped in seraphic dress <note>The "seraphic habit" refers to the robes of the Franciscan monks, who were known as the Seraphic Order.</note>.

->XCV.<-
Within the Spains a very powerful King shall come,
Subjugating the south by sea and land:
This evil shall be, lowering the crescent <note>The "croissant" or crescent was the heraldic symbol of the Ottoman Empire.</note>,
Clipping the wings of those of Friday <note>A reference to Muslims, for whom Friday is the primary day of congregational prayer.</note>.

->XCVI.<-
The religion named after the seas <note>The original "nom des mers" is ambiguous; it may refer to the "Marial" (Mary/Mer) or the "Pontifical" (Pontus/Sea) power.</note> will conquer,
Against the sect of the son of Adaluncatif <note>A cryptic name or anagram that has puzzled commentators for centuries.</note>:
The stubborn, lamented sect will fear,
Of the two wounded by Aleph and Aleph <note>Aleph is the first letter of the Hebrew alphabet. Its repetition suggests a coded name or a specific symbolic beginning.</note>.

->XCVII.<-
Triremes <term>An ancient type of galley with three banks of oars, still used in the Mediterranean during the 16th century.</term> full of captives of every age,
Good times turned to bad, sweetness to bitterness:
Prey to the Barbarians <note>In this era, "Barbarians" typically referred to the Barbary pirates of North Africa.</note>, they shall be too hasty...

# CENTURY X.

Desirous to see the feather lamenting in the wind. <note>This isolated line serves as a poetic fragment or a transition between the previous quatrains and the final sequence.</note>

### XCVIII.

The bright splendor for the joyous maiden
Will shine no longer; it will be without salt <note>In the 16th century, "salt" symbolized wisdom, wit, or the essential spirit that preserves a soul or a society.</note>:
With merchants, ruffians, and wolves, she will be odious,
All a muddled mess, a universal monster.

### XCIX.

In the end, the wolf, the lion, the ox, and the donkey,
The timid deer will be with the mastiffs:
The sweet manna shall no longer fall to them,
No more vigilance or guarding by the mastiffs. <note>This imagery evokes a breakdown of the natural order where predators and prey mingle in a state of neglect or shared exhaustion.</note>

### C.

The great empire shall be held by England,
The <term>pempotam</term> <note>original: "pempotam"; a word likely coined by the author from the Greek "pan" (all) and Latin "potens" (powerful), meaning an all-powerful ruler or sea-power.</note> for more than three hundred years:
Great armies passing by sea and land,
The Portuguese <note>original: "Lusitains"; the inhabitants of Lusitania, the Roman name for Portugal.</note> shall not be content with it.

->THE END.<-

->*of 1568.*<-

When the forked one <note>Likely referring to the Roman numeral "V".</note> shall be sustained by two poles <note>The numeral "II".</note>,
With six half-bodies <note>Commonly interpreted as six "C" shapes (the Roman numeral for 100).</note>, and six open scissors <note>Commonly interpreted as six "X" shapes (the Roman numeral for 10).</note>:
The most powerful Lord, heir to the toads <note>The toad was a legendary heraldic symbol of the Merovingian kings of France, used here to denote a French monarch.</note>,
Shall then subjugate the entire universe to himself.

# PROPHECIES OF
# Mr. Nostradamus.

## CENTURY XI.

### XCI.

Meynier, Manthi, and the third who shall come,
Plague and a new assault shall trouble the enclosed:
Within Aix and nearby places, fury shall bite,
The Phocaeans <note>original: "Phocens"; referring to the inhabitants of Marseille, which was founded by Phocaean Greeks.</note> shall come to double their woe.

### XCVII.

Through Villefranche, Mâcon shall be in disarray,
Within bundles of sticks <note>original: "fagots"; bundles of wood typically used for fuel.</note> shall soldiers be hidden:
A change of times in the spring for the King,
Near Chalon and Moulins, all hacked to pieces.

## Presages taken from those made by Mr. Nostradamus, in the years 1555 and following until 1567.

### 1555.

->From a presage on the said year.<-

->I.<-

The soul, touched by the foresight of the Divine Spirit,
Sees turmoil, famine, plague, and war running rampant:
Waters, droughts, earth and sea tinted with blood,
Peace, truce, Prelates to be born, and Princes to die.

*From the illuminating Epistle for the said year.*

->II.<-

The Tyrrhenian Sea <note>The part of the Mediterranean Sea off the western coast of Italy.</note> and the Ocean are guarded
By great Neptune and his trident-bearing soldiers:
Provence is made secure by the hand of the great Tende <note>Claude de Savoie, Count of Tende (1507–1566), who was the Governor of Provence.</note>,
Plus war in Narbonne by the heroic deeds of Vilars <note>Honorat II of Savoy, Marquis of Villars, a military commander and later Marshal of France.</note>.

<margin>January.</margin>
->III.<-

The large brass <note>original: *airain*; here referring to a great bronze church bell</note> that ordains the hours
Shall break upon the death of the Tyrant:
Tears, complaints and cries; waters and ice, the earth gives no bread,
V. S. C. <note>A cryptic abbreviation, possibly referring to *Vestra Sacra Caesarea* (Your Sacred Imperial Majesty) or *Veniat Salus Christi* (May the Salvation of Christ come).</note> peace, the army shall pass.

<summary>This page contains the opening prophecies of Nostradamus for the year 1555, written in his characteristic four-line rhyming verses (quatrains). The text predicts a year of natural disasters and political shifts, specifically mentioning regional defense in Southern France by local lords.</summary>

# PRESAGES.

Ten, twelve, thirteen, twenty, the Lady will speak,
The eldest will be corrupted by a woman:
Dijon, Guyenne <note>A historical province in southwest France</note> [face] hail, lightning strikes them,
The insatiable one sated with blood and wine.

->XI. September.<-

The sky weeps. It has been made to do that,
The sea prepares itself. Hannibal <note>Often used by the author to symbolize a cunning or North African military leader</note> uses his ruses:
Denis anchors <note>original: *mouille*; can also mean to get wet</note>. The fleet <note>original: *claſſe*, from the Latin *classis*</note> delays. Do not keep silent,
He did not know the secret, and [knows] what you amuse yourself with.

->XII. October.<-

Venus [and] Neptune will pursue the enterprise,
Pressed [and] pensive. The opponents [are] troubled:
The fleet in the Adriatic <note>original: *Adrie*</note>. Cities near the Thames,
The fourth noise wounds the sleepers by night.

->XIII. November.<-

The great one of the sky will give under the Cape <note>original: *la Cape*; may refer to a constellation, a religious garment (cope), or a specific location</note>,
Help. Adria <note>the Adriatic region</note> makes an offer at the gate:
He who can will save himself from the dangers,
At night the wounded Great One pursues the chest <note>or coffer</note>.

->XIIII. December.<-

The Gate exclaims [at what is] too fraudulent and feigned,
The mouth wide open, a condition of peace:
The Rhône [river] like crystal. Water, snow, ice tinted,
Death, death, wind. By rain, the burden is broken.

->1557.<-
->XV. January.<-

The unworthy one, adorned, will fear the great furnace,
